#5220. Market Bloodbath: Cryptocurrency Industry Faces $435 Million in Liquidations

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The crypto market is in for another bumpy ride as financial events coincide with a spike in volatility, and around $435 million in positions have been liquidated over the past 24 hours. Bitcoin (BTC) and Ethereum (ETH) are taking the biggest hit, and leveraged traders keep losing money on major exchanges.

According to CoinGlass, total liquidations reached $440 million, hitting 91,821 traders. Shorts accounted for $171 million. Longs took the bigger hit with $269 million, or 61.2% of the total, which shows that most of the pain fell on traders betting on a rise.

Bitcoin led with $163.8 million in liquidations, followed by Ethereum at $68 million. Other coins added $50 million in total liquidations, which makes the distribution of liquidations somewhat equal here.

Bitcoin's daily chart looks somewhat fragile as BTC is trading around $78,006 after a pullback from its October highs near $87,000, which is an unfortunate sign, suggesting the recovery continuation is unlikely here.

BTC is now testing a key support zone between $76,000 and $78,000, where the longer-term moving averages collide. In a case where resistance is broken, new support should arise around $74,000 and possibly $70,000.

The closest resistance is near $80,000-$82,000, with multiple moving averages forming a trend, which will act as a resistance with Bitcoin's ascending movement.

Momentum indicators prompt further weakness, with the Relative Strength Index (RSI) slipping towards the lower half of its range. Bullish momentum is fading, but the market isn't extremely oversold yet.

Hyperliquid saw around $39.84 million in liquidations over four hours, or 40.8% of the total for that period. Binance came next with $29 million, and OKX recorded $9.09 million.

The largest single liquidation reported was $5.03 million on Binance's ETHUSDT market.

The liquidation numbers don't prove that the outside events caused the sell-off. Traders cutting risks and high leverage could make the swings even bigger. If Bitcoin holds the $76,000 support zone, the market might calm down, but unfortunately, there's another big wave of liquidations sitting just around the corner in case of a breakdown.

#5219. Copilot AI Predicts an Explosive End to 2026 for Hyperliquid (HYPE)

Microsoft’s Copilot AI predicts that Hyperliquid could continue its bullish momentum into 2027, believing a euphoric HYPE rally is near

A potentially explosive development for Hyperliquid could hit by January 1, 2027, as Microsoft’s Copilot AI predicts that in an intentionally extreme, hypothetical scenario built around a landmark catalyst, HYPE crypto could soar to new highs over Q4.

The scenario envisions Hyperliquid as the primary high-performance liquidity and matching engine for a new generation of regulated on-chain prediction markets and event contracts.

(SOURCE: Copilot AI Predicts Parabolic Move for Hyperliquid)

In this context, major platforms and institutions would route significant volumes of prediction-market and event-based trading through Hyperliquid’s infrastructure. This would position HYPE as a vital component in one of the fastest-growing segments of decentralized finance.

As of today (November 4), the Hyperliquid token is trading at around $86, up nearly +4% on the day following a weekly move of over +6%. The HYPE market cap sits at $20.7Bn, making it the 11th largest cryptocurrency by market cap, per CoinGecko data.

On higher timeframes, HYPE has been consolidating in the $82–$87 range after pulling back from its recent all-time high of around $98. Price continues to hold above key intermediate support but faces resistance in the $80–$85 zone.

In a typical bull market, a sustained breakout above the $100–$120 range would indicate intermediate strength and set the stage for significant new price movements.

In an extreme scenario where prediction-market infrastructure sees widespread adoption, surpassing previous highs could lead to strong measured-move extensions and multi-cycle Fibonacci projections from a longer-term base.

Currently, momentum indicators are in neutral to corrective territory, suggesting considerable room to advance into the extreme overbought levels often seen during parabolic moves.

Key near-term support levels are around $74–$75 and $68–$70. Holding these levels would help maintain the broader market structure while the market adjusts to major narrative developments.

From an economic perspective, a noticeable shift by major central banks towards more accommodative monetary policies, along with sustained liquidity conditions, would encourage risk-taking across various markets.

In the cryptocurrency space, ongoing inflows into Bitcoin and Ethereum ETFs, greater institutional adoption of decentralized derivatives, increased trading volumes in prediction markets, and a revival of risk appetite for altcoins would create a favorable environment. This scenario would particularly benefit high-performance trading platforms like Hyperliquid.

In this highly speculative scenario, if a full-blown crypto bull market returns alongside favorable external conditions, Hyperliquid (HYPE) could potentially reach a price range between $500 and $900, with an optimistic target of over $1,400 by January 1, 2027.

This is purely speculation and should be treated as entertainment rather than a realistic baseline outlook. Achieving this would require exceptional coordination among prediction market platforms, regulators, and the Hyperliquid ecosystem, which currently seems unlikely.

#5217. XRP Whales Reach Highest Supply Share in 4 Years; Can Price Reclaim $3 Level?

XRP recovered slightly by Tuesday’s close after falling the day before, trading near $1.31 as traders assessed fresh signs of whale accumulation amid lingering selling pressure.

In a Tuesday post on X, Santiment reported that wallets holding at least 10 million XRP now control 46.87 billion tokens, about 68.5% of the cryptocurrency’s supply. That marks the largest share this group has held since November 2022.

“The key signal is that these massive holders have kept increasing exposure even while XRP struggles near $1.29,” Santiment wrote, suggesting that large investors remain willing to accumulate despite the token’s weakness.

The figures mark a shift from an earlier warning by analyst Ali Martinez, who cited a decline in wallets holding between 10 million and 100 million XRP, from 351 to 339 since November 2022. He also noted that exchange balances rose from 11.003 billion to about 11.05 billion XRP, which could partly explain the ongoing price stagnation. Martinez warned that continued selling by large holders could send XRP toward $0.98.

However, Santiment’s latest figures suggest that accumulation among the largest wallets may be strengthening even as the price struggles.

“Large stakeholders accumulating through weakness suggests conviction remains strong beneath the price action,” Santiment stated.

The analytics platform identified $1.30 as the immediate pivot, with $1.90 as a stronger level to confirm a potential recovery.

Meanwhile, CryptoQuant analyst Darkfost reported a sharp drop in XRP derivatives exposure, potentially easing some of the pressure from leveraged positions. According to him, Binance open interest fell to $445 million, below its 180-day average of approximately $460 million.

Since September 22, the metric has declined nearly 28%, while XRP’s price has dropped 17% over the same period. He attributed the decline partly to falling prices, which reduce the value of outstanding positions, and to liquidations triggered during the sell-off.

“This deleveraging dynamic is a rather positive signal as it suggests that pressure on XRP futures is gradually easing,” the analyst said.

Moreover, Darkfost cited previous episodes in which falling open interest preceded substantial XRP rallies, suggesting a similar scenario could unfold.

“We saw this in mid-September, when XRP gained 32% in the following period, and after the mid-August episode, which was followed by a 72% rally.” He added.

Martinez also identified a potential technical catalyst for a rebound. The analyst said the TD Sequential indicator had flashed a buy signal on XRP’s three-day chart, noting that the previous three comparable signals preceded rebounds of 31%, 24% and 18%. However, the setup depends on XRP holding its support level.

“Hold it, and XRP could rebound toward $1.40. Lose it while whales continue taking profits, and $0.98 could be next,” Martinez said.

Elsewhere, analyst Joep Gerla highlighted XRP’s prolonged consolidation as a possible precursor to a larger advance. He compared the current price structure with previous consolidation periods that preceded rallies of 63,617% in 2017–2018, 1,687% in 2020–2021 and 901% in 2024–2025.

“XRP has been in prolonged consolidation, a structure that has preceded major expansion phases in previous cycles,” Gerla wrote. “The edge goes to those who stay patient before the breakout.”

At press time, XRP was trading at $1.31, up 2.13% in the past 24 hours.

#5216. 18 New XRP Millionaires Emerge in 72 Hours: Does It Signal a Price Bottom?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The current decline in XRP's market value has not led to loss-taking by large stakeholders — on the contrary, a phase of aggressive accumulation has unfolded in the market. According to data from the analytical platform Santiment, an absolute record for the current year has been set on the XRP Ledger network: 2,147 wallets now hold a balance of 1 million tokens or more.

The main inflow of capital came during the price correction period, when the asset's market price dropped to the $1.27 mark.

The main activity of large capital unfolded at the end of October. The dynamics with which investors are forming large positions points to a rigid strategic calculation: over the past ten days, 28 new addresses have attained millionaire status, and 18 of them were opened during the final three days of the reporting period.

Unlike speculative capital oriented toward quick profit, large holders traditionally use phases of price weakness for buying. The growth in the number of millionaires at such a moment confirms that large balances are being systematically increased rather than liquidated.

Such divergences between a falling chart and an inflow of institutional capital often precede the formation of a long-term market bottom.

Is this surge a signal that the price has found a bottom? Analysts identify three clear price thresholds that will give a precise answer to this question in the coming days:

• The $1.20 – $1.25 zone (Foundation): A critical support line and the main indicator of a potential bottom. If the price holds in this range and the number of millionaire wallets remains above 2,140, the long-term growth scenario will remain in force.

• The $1.30 milestone (Psychology): A local resistance level, a return above which is necessary to confirm a reversal and restore optimism in the mass market.

• The $1.40 level (Confirmation): Consolidation of the price above this mark amid a continuing inflow of large holders will prove that the bottom has been definitively passed and that capital is ready to drive the asset toward new highs.

Thus, the sustainability of XRP's current bullish setup depends directly on the ability of large capital to hold key support levels during the coming market movements.

#5213. Solana Co-Founder Names Biggest Threat to Crypto

Solana co-founder Anatoly Yakovenko said phishing and social engineering attacks are the biggest threat to crypto and advised investors to worry more about social engineering attacks than about AI or quantum computers breaking crypto security.

"Phishing and social engineering is the biggest threat to crypto. Not AI breaking math or quantum computers," he wrote on X.

The Solana co-founder also suggested having a separate iOS device with Apple's Lockdown Mode turned on and using a multisignature crypto wallet.

This is one of the cheapest ways for investors to safeguard their digital assets, according to Yakovenko.

The Solana co-founder's statement highlights a fundamental weakness in crypto security: hackers don't need to break blockchain security to steal digital assets.

What they can do is fool crypto holders into giving them access to their assets.

Phishing involves using malicious emails, sites or messages to trick people into revealing sensitive information such as crypto wallet recovery phrases, account information, etc.

Social engineering is a broader term that describes attacks in which hackers manipulate victims into doing something that can compromise their security.

Yakovenko's point becomes crystal clear with the recent massive hack at Binance.

The world's largest crypto exchange, Binance, faced a security breach on Oct. 29 that affected around $6.34 billion in digital assets.

According to Binance's Nov. 3 investigation update, the hackers carried out a sophisticated social engineering attack, which included fake job interviews to compromise Binance employees.

A Binance software engineer was targeted by people pretending to be representatives of a fake company. The Binance employee went through some fake job interviews. The employee was convinced to download what seemed to be a legitimate technical assignment.

But the downloaded file contained malicious code that helped the hackers compromise the employee's computer.

Then, the hackers used the compromised devices, stolen credentials and an internal AI tool to access Binance's wallet infrastructure.

Eventually, this helped the hackers execute unauthorized crypto transfers from the exchange's hot wallets.

The Security Alliance (SEAL) released a report on Nov. 4 stating that its security team handled 99 incidents from Oct. 28 to Nov. 3.

In three of those incidents involving fraudulent customer support calls, victims lost roughly $1.57 million and 1.12 BTC from Oct. 28 to Nov. 3.

One of the scams involved fake Apple support representatives stealing a victim's Apple ID credentials. The victim had saved their crypto recovery phrase in cloud storage. Another victim shared their wallet recovery phrase during a fake Telegram support call.

The group also tracked 5,137 phishing domains during this reporting period.

#5212. Options Traders Drop Their Election Hedges as BTC Retakes $78,500, and Zcash Leverage Jumps 28% Before Its Upgrade

Bitcoin news: volatility hits a post-hack low as BTC nears $78,600, and Zcash leverage jumps 28% before NU7.

In Bitcoin news today, options traders dropped the hedges they had bought for the US midterms, pulling Bitcoin’s implied volatility to its lowest level since the Binance hack as BTC USD price climbed back to $78,570.

Deribit’s DVOL index, which tracks how much Bitcoin traders expect the price to swing over the next 30 days, fell as low as 37.03 this morning. It stood at 40.76 at midday on Tuesday, as Americans started voting.

The price has recovered with it. Bitcoin trades at $78,570 on Binance, 3.7% above the $75,736 low it hit as results came in overnight.

Altcoin traders are taking more risk. Open interest in Zcash futures on OKX jumped 28% in 24 hours to $196 million, a day and a half before the network’s NU7 upgrade.

So with the election out of the way, can Bitcoin get back above $80,000, and which altcoins are bouncing the most?

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

A calmer month, with a tilt to the upside. The midterms never scared Bitcoin options traders the way the Binance hack did: the DVOL index peaked at 44.25 four hours after the hack, and only reached 40.89 around the vote.

The bets on Deribit lean bullish. Open interest stands at 193,024 BTC in calls against 132,544 BTC in puts, a put-to-call ratio of 0.69, and calls outnumbered puts in the last 24 hours of trading too.

For the November 27 expiry, the biggest single position is 4,484 BTC of $90,000 calls. The biggest put sits at $75,000, with 2,880 BTC, right under this week’s low.

Polymarket traders see the near-term targets the same way:

The futures market is less sure. On OKX, Bitcoin open interest rose 4.2% in 24 hours to $3.86 billion, but the share of accounts betting on a rise fell, with the long-to-short ratio dropping to 1.88 from 2.74. Funding on Hyperliquid sits at the default 0.01% every eight hours, so no one is paying up to stay long yet.

ETF investors haven’t come back yet. US spot Bitcoin ETFs had net outflows of $115.56 million on Tuesday, November 3, bringing 30-day outflows to $1.32 billion. Spot Ether funds lost another $85.35 million.

It has the floor it needed. Bitcoin’s overnight wick to $75,736 bounced just under its 200-day EMA at $76,530, and it hasn’t closed a day below that average since the Binance hack.

The ceiling is close. Bitcoin is $1,500 below the 50-day EMA at $80,076, the level Polymarket traders give an 85.5% chance of being reached this month.

Prices and lows from Binance, open interest from OKX, both at about 11:45 UTC.

The crypto market cap declined by more than $120 billion on Thursday to $2.57 trillion.

Bitcoin, ETH, XRP, and Solana led the broader cryptocurrency selloff over the past 24 hours.

Despite the renewed selling pressure on Thursday, a possible recovery is anticipated in the near future

As Bitcoin (BTC), Ethereum (ETH), XRP, and Solana (SOL) capitulated on November 5, 2026, the crypto market capitalization flash-crashed by more than $120 billion.

The crypto market cap fell to $2.57 trillion at the time of reporting, down from $2.69 trillion on Wednesday. As a result, the total cryptocurrency market cap declined by roughly 4.46%, according to data Coinpedia analyzed from CoinMarketCap.

Crypto market cap change on November 5. Source: CoinMarketCap

The free-fall was driven by a significant selloff in top crypto assets. At the time of writing, Bitcoin, ETH, XRP, and SOL had shed 4.46%, 6.68%, 4.45%, and 5.9%, respectively.

Top 7 crypto assets by market cap on Thursday. Source: CoinMarketCap

The cryptocurrency market faced heightened selling pressure following renewed geopolitical tensions amid a broader bear market in the artificial intelligence (AI) stocks.

Over the past 24 hours, the Nasdaq-100 Index (NDX), an equity benchmark tracking the 100 largest non-financial companies listed on the Nasdaq Stock Market, plummeted to its lowest in 196 days.

As such, the cryptocurrency market faced increased selling pressure catalyzed by broader macro headwinds.

Most importantly, the crypto market experienced a long squeeze, a situation in which falling prices force long holders to sell, thereby accelerating the decline. Over the past 24 hours, the cryptocurrencies’ derivatives had recorded around $1.06 billion in total liquidations, with $934.73 million involving long trades, as per metrics from CoinGlass.

Cryptocurrency liquidation heat map on Thursday. Source: CoinGlass

Even though the cryptocurrency market faced increased selloff fueled by a possible AI bubble burst, a possible bull run is expected before the end of this year.

#5208. Can You Put Crypto Mining in Space? Elon Musk's Data Center Idea Can Be Used in Crypto

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Elon Musk did what he does best on X: fresh speculations via post on November 4, saying: “Data centers in space look pretty appealing right now.”

Musk was talking about data centers, but the idea can be used in crypto too: could Bitcoin mining rigs work in orbit, powered only by the Sun? Technically, they should, why not? Economically, however, it's getting a bit more complicated.

Space has one big advantage: plenty of solar energy. Unlike mining farms on Earth, orbital mining stations could use large solar ray to make their own electricity without any power grid. Satellites today could get constant powering from sunlight, leading to a lower battery consumption.

Data centers in space looking pretty appealing right now

In theory, Bitcoin ASIC miners could then run around the clock with very low or non-existent ongoing electricity costs. The problem is that solar panels, batteries, power electronics and would all have to be launched into orbit–that's the part where problems arise.

Space might look like the perfect place to cool mining equipment, since the cosmic background is extremely cold. Unfortunately, it's not so simple and the vacuum in space actually makes cooling harder than on earth.

Here, mining rigs get rid of heat with fans, air conditioning or liquid cooling, in orbit, there's no air at all that will be able to push the air away. So the only option out there seems to be the liquid cooling, large radiator panels which should be able to release heat as infrared radiation.

According to Boston Consulting Group (BCG), a 100-kilowatt orbital data center could need around a whopping 400 square meters of radiators. But apart from technical challenges, we should always keep in mind the profitability. Getting equipment to space in the first place is what makes the whole idea questionable.

According to BCG, launching to orbit currently costs around $1,500 per kilogram. That means, a one-ton mining setup would cost around $1.5 million just to launch, excluding hardware, cooling, solar arrays and maintenance that will cost just as much if not more.

You also can't ignore space radiation, equipment failures and limited chances for repairs that add even more costs at the top of it. The only thing that can somehow make math look better is more efficient ASIC chips and ways of delivering goods to space at the fraction of today's costs.

Thankfully, Bitcoin miners need little to no bandwidth, so the connection issue is somewhat solved on its own.

Physically, mining Bitcoin in space is possible: is it justifiable financially though? Not really. Reusable rockets, lightweight cooling and other things might make it more financially viable, but as of today, we should be sticking with good old mining rigs in huge hangars on Earth.

#5207. Top Wallet-as-a-Service Providers for Ethereum in 2026

Disclaimer: The below article is sponsored, and the views in it do not represent those of ZyCrypto. Readers should conduct independent research before taking any actions related to the project mentioned in this piece. This article should not be regarded as investment advice.

How Aligned, Privy, Dynamic, Turnkey, and Magic compare

A small group of established providers leads wallet-as-a-service solutions (WaaS) on Ethereum, including Aligned, Privy, Dynamic, Turnkey, and Magic. All are non-custodial and primarily compete on four things: how they secure the key, how they handle accounts and gas, how much of the stack they cover, and who owns the company.

For a consumer app or a financial institution building on Ethereum, the wallet is the integration you most want to get right the first time. It’s what users touch directly and trust with their funds, and it signs every transaction they make. It’s also the decision that compounds: once people hold balances, switching providers means migrating live accounts, so getting it right early pays off most.

Today’s leading WaaS providers look almost identical on the surface. They’re all non-custodial, and they all spare users the crypto mechanics: no seed phrase, no gas token, no contract prompts to approve. The differences that shape an integration sit underneath: the key-security model, how accounts and gas work, how much of the stack comes from one vendor, and who owns that vendor.

The field of credible wallet providers is small but far from uniform. Each was built around a different priority, and they overlap enough to blur together. Understanding what each one is keeps the biggest name from standing in for the best fit.

Aligned: Independent, founded by the team behind LambdaClass, and the only provider in the group focused exclusively on Ethereum: it runs on Ethereum L1 and on ZK rollups built with ethrex, while the others prioritize multichain reach. Every account is an EIP-7702 smart account that requires two signatures: one from the user’s key on their device and one from Aligned’s backend. It ships as part of a larger stack, with the rollup, proof aggregation, and interoperability all coming from the same team. Sovra runs identity for 8M+ people on it.

Privy (Stripe): The most widely deployed of the group by self-reported counts, with 200M+ accounts and customers like Hyperliquid and Ramp. It splits each key into two Shamir shares, both held on Privy’s side (one inside a secure enclave), and reassembles the key only inside the enclave. Its default EOA wallet can be upgraded in place through EIP-7702, or act as the signer of a separate ERC-4337 smart wallet. It carries several independent audits plus SOC 2 Type I and Type II.

Dynamic (Fireblocks): As much a login-and-wallet-connection platform as an embedded wallet, with a wide range of sign-in methods and connectors to 800+ external wallets. That fits apps where plenty of users already have a wallet, alongside those who need one made. Its keys use threshold-signature MPC. Customers have included Kraken and Ondo.

Turnkey: The signing and key-management foundation other wallets build on. It generates and uses raw private keys inside secure enclaves, with a policy engine that checks every signature. Of the enclave-based providers, Turnkey is the one that lets you verify the enclave itself: reproducible builds and remote attestation confirm the exact code that touched the key. Independent, founded by an ex-Coinbase Custody team. Customers include Bridge and Polymarket.

Magic: The oldest of the group, launched in 2018 and known for email-first onboarding. It protects keys with a mix of secure enclaves, a cloud key-management service, and sharding, and it holds the widest set of compliance certifications in the group: SOC 2, SOC 3, and ISO 27001. Its wallet business is being acquired by Payward, Kraken’s parent company, in a deal agreed in July 2026.

Most top WaaS solutions today are non-custodial, meaning they can’t move users’ funds without their consent. But that label covers a wide range: how much you trust the provider depends on how it handles the private key, and that trust underpins what you can promise users about their money. The clearest divide is whether the full key is ever assembled in one place, and it splits these solutions into two camps.

All three bring the full key together inside secure hardware, and they differ only on when. Privy splits it into two Shamir shares (not MPC), both held on its side, and reassembles them in a TEE at signing. Magic does the same with a TEE, a cloud key-management service, and sharding. Turnkey skips reassembly, keeping raw keys inside secure enclaves full-time behind a policy engine. Either way, the enclave exposes the full key, so its integrity is the security boundary.

Each gives you a different way to trust that boundary. Turnkey makes the enclave verifiable through open-source enclave code, reproducible builds, and remote attestation, so you can confirm the exact code handling the key. Privy leans on outside audits from Cure53, Zellic, and Doyensec, and Magic on the widest set of certifications in the group, SOC 2, SOC 3, and ISO 27001.

Dynamic and Aligned take the enclave out of the picture: no single key can move funds on its own, and they get there two ways. Dynamic uses threshold-signature MPC, where independent parties each hold a share and sign jointly, so no machine, Dynamic’s included, ever holds a spendable key. Aligned uses two separate keys and requires both to sign. The user’s key stays on their device (a WebAuthn passkey, a P-256 key, or an ECDSA key), Aligned’s key stays on its backend, and the account contract checks both signatures on-chain before it executes anything. Passkeys are verified on-chain as P-256 signatures inside the WebAuthn envelope. A breach of Aligned’s backend doesn’t move funds, and neither does a stolen device on its own.

The trade-off to plan for is availability: a signature needs a second party online. The two handle that differently. Dynamic’s 2-of-3 option lets any two of the three shares sign, so one being offline won’t lock the wallet. Aligned’s 2-of-2 doesn’t have that cushion: every transaction needs Aligned’s co-signer, so access depends on its backend staying reachable. For lost devices, Aligned offers optional guardian recovery: the guardian is set per app, and the timelock per account. The guardian can add a new key to an account, but that key can’t sign until a timelock expires (1 to 14 days, fixed when the account is created), which leaves a window to cancel an unwanted recovery. Accounts created without a timelock have no guardian.

One more point of trust sits above the accounts themselves. Aligned accounts route through a beacon contract, one per app, and the beacon’s owner can rotate the signers and point accounts to a new implementation without touching each one. That makes upgrades and key rotation simple to operate, and it means teams should know who controls the beacon for their app.

On its own, an Ethereum account is bare: a user needs ETH to do anything, every action is a separate approval, and losing the key means losing the funds. Account abstraction lifts those limits by giving the account programmable logic, so a product can cover gas for users, batch several steps into one confirmation, and offer recovery. All of them do this. They differ in the underlying standard, and providers are split between them.

ERC-4337 is the longest-running route, and Privy’s smart wallets build on ERC-4337. It works entirely at the application layer: user actions become “UserOperation” objects that a separate mempool and “bundler” services collect, batch, and submit through a shared EntryPoint contract, with an optional paymaster covering gas. Because it needs no change to Ethereum itself, it went live on Ethereum mainnet in March 2023 and now has the deepest audit and tooling history of any account-abstraction approach.

That maturity comes with operational overhead. Each user needs a separate smart-contract account deployed and maintained, and much of the market reaches 4337 through shared third-party middleware such as ZeroDev. It’s a well-worn path, and it also puts a common external layer in the critical signing path for many apps at once, which is worth weighing at institutional scale.

Aligned builds on EIP-7702, a 2025 protocol change that landed in Ethereum’s Pectra upgrade. It lets an ordinary account (an EOA) point to contract code and run it directly, so a user’s existing address gains smart-account behavior without deploying a second account alongside it. Aligned uses 7702 on its own, with no 4337 EntryPoint, bundler, or paymaster: its backend submits each transaction and pays the gas, so users never need to hold ETH or the chain’s native token. The result is leaner: one address, nothing extra to deploy or fund, and no third-party middleware between the user and the chain. Because it depends on 7702, it runs only on chains that have adopted Pectra.

EIP-7702 and ERC-4337 aren’t mutually exclusive: Privy signs 7702 authorizations for any implementation, Dynamic and Magic now offer 7702-based smart accounts, and Turnkey, as a pure signer, handles both transaction types and leaves the account model to you. Which one fits depends on whether a team wants established 4337 tooling or a simpler account with fewer moving parts.

Providers also differ in how much of the wallet you build yourself. The options fall on three levels, from a bare signing primitive you wrap in your own code to a full stack that arrives assembled. Where a provider sits decides how many vendors you take on and how independent the pieces of your stack stay.

Turnkey sits lowest, by design. It hands you a signing and key-management foundation and leaves the account-abstraction layer, gas handling, and smart-account logic for you to build. That’s the most work and the most control. Your wallet logic stays independent of any one chain or vendor, and signing sits behind a boundary you can attest. Teams with their own engineers often start here for that reason.

Privy, Dynamic, and Magic sit in the middle, as full embedded-wallet SDKs. They handle onboarding, key security, and gas, while you bring the chain and settle wherever you like. Dynamic leans hardest into authentication and wallet connection, with 800+ external-wallet connectors, so it suits apps where many users show up with a wallet already.

Aligned sits at the other end and sells the most in one piece: the wallet plus a rollup and proof aggregation, with interoperability, all from one team. Its ZK rollups are built with ethrex, the Rust Ethereum client from LambdaClass, and settle on Ethereum. That folds several procurement and integration decisions into one, and removes the seams between those layers.

It also concentrates your dependencies. The wallet, chain, and settlement all come from one provider, so they move together on a single roadmap and a single uptime, and more of your stack rides on one vendor. An institution gets one supplier to vet, and one point of dependence to weigh in return.

A wallet provider is a long-term dependency, so the decision comes down to a few things each team has to settle: how much to trust a provider with key handling, how much of the account and gas experience to hand off, and how much of the stack to source from one vendor.

The demo won’t decide it for you, because they all deliver the same clean experience: no seed phrase, no gas token, a smooth login. Their real differences sit underneath, in the two choices hardest to reverse once users hold funds: how the key is secured, and how much of the product leans on a single company. Past that, the best fit depends on what you’re building and what stage it’s at.

Provider details, figures, and ownership information were checked against public sources as of November 2026 and may change; teams should confirm them with each provider before choosing one.

#5206. Core Scientific to Wind Down Bitcoin Mining By Q4 2026 as AI Takes Over

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Rizwan is an experienced Crypto journalist with almost half a decade of experience covering everything related to the growing crypto industry — from price analysis to blockchain disruption. During this period, he’s authored more than 3,000 news articles for Coinpedia News.

Rizwan is an experienced Crypto journalist with almost half a decade of experience covering everything related to the growing crypto industry — from price analysis to blockchain disruption. During this period, he’s authored more than 3,000 news articles for Coinpedia News.

Rizwan is an experienced Crypto journalist with almost half a decade of experience covering everything related to the growing crypto industry — from price analysis to blockchain disruption. During this period, he’s authored more than 3,000 news articles for Coinpedia News.

Core Scientific plans to fully exit Bitcoin mining operations by the end of Q4 2026.

AI data-center workloads can generate far more value from the same power capacity than Bitcoin mining.

Riot Platforms, MARA, HIVE and Hut 8 are also shifting toward AI and HPC infrastructure.

Core Scientific, one of the biggest names in Bitcoin mining, plans to wind down its mining operations by Q4 2026. It is not alone. Other major mining firms, including MARA Holdings, Riot Platforms, and HIVE Digital, have already begun shifting away from traditional Bitcoin mining.

But what is driving these major companies to shift their mining businesses? The reasons may surprise you.

During its Q3 2026 earnings call, Core Scientific confirmed that it expects to wind down its self-mining operations by 2026. From 2027, the company will focus on providing data center space for AI and high-performance computing.

The shift is already visible in its financial results.

Core Scientific shared its Q3 2026 results this morning. Key updates:– Data center revenue grew nearly 60% from last quarter to $215.4M, driven by continued capacity delivery to our customers.– As of September 30, 2026, we are billing for 485MW of capacity, generating over… pic.twitter.com/IIGp6p1tzm

Core Scientific generated $215.4 million in colocation revenue during Q3, up nearly 60% from the previous quarter. Colocation accounted for 93% of total revenue, while Bitcoin self-mining generated just $12.3 million. Adjusted EBITDA also jumped to $133.6 million, pushing the margin to 57%.

The company has already reduced its active mining fleet by nearly 50% from the end of 2025.

It even paid $41.9 million to end its Bitcoin mining equipment agreement with Block and Proto, ending future equipment deliveries.

The economics of Bitcoin mining have become increasingly difficult for large public miners.

Following the April 2024 halving, the block reward was cut from 6.25 BTC to 3.125 BTC. For public mining companies, the average cost of producing one Bitcoin has climbed to around $88.341. When Bitcoin trades at or below $80,000, miners can lose money on every coin they produce. Even when Bitcoin briefly rises above $80,000, profit margins remain very thin.

These changes have put pressure on mining profits. Core Scientific said weaker mining conditions contributed to a $266.5 million loss in value on its mining-related assets earlier this year.

Electricity costs are also pushing miners to consider other businesses. According to an energy infrastructure analyst, one megawatt of power can generate roughly $1.9 million over five years from Bitcoin mining, compared with about $7.9 million from AI data-center workloads.

This makes AI data centers a more profitable option for companies that already have large power facilities. Instead of using that electricity to mine Bitcoin, they can rent their facilities to AI companies through long-term contracts.

Core Scientific is already doing that. Its AMD and Neocloud agreement covers about 530 MW, while AMD also has reservation rights for another 1,925 MW of potential capacity.

Several other major miners are following a similar path. MARA Holdings sold about 15,133 BTC for roughly $1.1 billion in March and cut its workforce by 15% as it redirected capital toward balance-sheet strengthening and other opportunities.

Riot Platforms is taking the AI route even further. It signed a 20-year lease for 191 MW of critical IT capacity with a major AI lab, with an estimated $9.1 billion in contract revenue over the initial term.

HIVE is winding down its Bitcoin mining operations in Sweden while expanding its AI and HPC infrastructure in Canada.

The shift is becoming significant across the sector. At least 35 EH/s of computing power is scheduled to leave publicly listed Bitcoin miners as companies redirect capacity toward AI and high-performance computing.

#5204. Bitcoin Back Above $80,000 as $468 Million of Shorts Get Wiped Out: Can BTC Clear Its Pre-Hack High?

Bitcoin news: BTC closed above $80,000 for the first time since the Binance hack as $468M of shorts were wiped out. Will it hold?

Bitcoin news turned to a short squeeze on Wednesday, November 4, as BTC closed above $80,000 for the first time since the October 29 Binance hack. A jump at the US stock market open forced traders to close $468.5 million of short bets. Bitcoin trades at $80,550, up 2.7% in 24 hours. ETH USD price did better, rising 4.4% to $2,427.

The move came in two steps. BTC went from $78,321 at 14:30 UTC, when New York opened, to $79,559 within 15 minutes, then pushed to $81,196 by 15:15 UTC. Earlier the same day, it had dropped to $75,736, so Wednesday’s range was more than 7%.

Most of the traders caught out were betting on a fall. That matters, because a rally fed by forced short covering can fade once the buying stops.

Bitcoin is resilient. pic.twitter.com/qUHWw2uwnt

— CZ 🔶 BNB (@cz_binance) November 4, 2026

Discover: Cboe and Citadel Sue to Stop US Stock Perps Hours After the CFTC Approved Them: Why Hyperliquid’s $5 Billion-a-Day Market Has the Most at Stake

CoinGlass counted $715.6 million of crypto futures liquidations over 24 hours, and 65.5% of them, or $468.5 million, were shorts. Some 99,879 traders were liquidated.

Bitcoin led with $267.1 million liquidated, and 93.4% of that was shorts. Ether followed with $197.5 million, 58.1% of it shorts. Binance handled 39.3% of all liquidations. The largest single order went the other way. A $38 million ETH long was closed on HTX at $2,429.33, close to where ETH trades now.

On OKX, BTC futures open interest rose to $3.88 billion during the jump, then fell to $3.74 billion by midnight UTC. A drop like that suggests traders closed positions rather than opened new ones.

The crowd has not turned bullish. The OKX ratio of BTC accounts holding longs to those holding shorts fell from 1.84 at 11:00 UTC on Wednesday to 1.52 on Thursday morning, so more accounts are now betting against the rally.

BTC sits right on top of its 20-day EMA at $80,006, and its 50-day EMA at $80,166, so the $80,000 area is now both a round number and the first support.

The $81,971 level is where BTC traded before news of the Binance hack broke. Reclaiming it would erase the hack-day drop on the chart.

Options traders are calm about it. Deribit’s DVOL index, which tracks expected Bitcoin volatility over 30 days, eased to 36.9 from 38 on Wednesday.

The next Bitcoin news to watch is whether spot buyers step in now that the short covering has cooled. Without them, $80,000 is the line that decides whether this was a breakout or only a squeeze.

#5203. Dogecoin Contributor Shares Key Safety Reminder Amid Binance Withdrawal Resumption

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Dogecoin community member Mishaboar has shared a fresh safety reminder to DOGE holders as cryptocurrency withdrawals resume on Binance following the exchange's recent security incident.

Binance began restoring withdrawals in phases on November 1, 2026, with that of Dogecoin already restored. Binance has also published a cryptographic Proof of Reserves for selected assets, including Dogecoin, based on its October 31 snapshot.

A few updates on the Binance situation since my last post. - The Hack Binance has finally published its initial technical findings, and, as we had expected, the attack was considerably more complicated than the original explanation involving a compromised devops laptop. According to Binance, the attackers targeted multiple engineers through malicious software, including an earlier attempt involving a tampered devtool installer and a fake job interview that delivered malware. They then exploited an internal AI tool and stolen deployment credentials to gain access to Binance's wallet-signing infrastructure. On Ethereum, malicious EIP-7702 authorizations allowed the attackers to continue moving funds even after Binance had suspended normal withdrawals. It's difficult to understand why the signing controls allowed these authorizations in the first place. I am curious to know how it is possible the authorizations were not detected more quickly, and why revoking them took so long on wallets holding such gigantic amounts. Binance says it took almost four hours to fully contain the attack. It will be good to see independent reviews from competent security analysts. - Withdrawals/Deposits (after withdrawals reopened) According to figures shared by Binance co-CEO Richard Teng and reported by CoinDesk, approximately $10.97 billion was withdrawn during the first 46 hours after withdrawals reopened, against $5.69 billion in deposits. That leaves approximately $5.28 billion in net outflows, as of early November 3. Withdrawal activity was particularly intense on Sunday, but appears to have slowed down since. Binance claims that hourly deposits had started exceeding withdrawals by the end of that period. - The Money Binance reported approximately $2.85 billion frozen or intercepted in its November 3 update. Once again, remember that frozen funds are NOT necessarily recovered funds. Binance also says it has replaced the vast majority of affected assets using its own resources, including SAFU and insurance funds (see my previous update for considerations about this). The published replenishment percentages have been somewhat inconsistent, however. And once again, replacing stolen assets is NOT the same as recovering them from the attackers. Using SAFU and the Futures Insurance Fund to cover these losses also means that the risk profile of holding funds on the exchange may have shifted considerably. As exchanges operate largely within black boxes, it is difficult to assess this at this point. - Withdrawals and Proof of Reserves Binance has published a cryptographic Proof of Reserves for ten selected assets (including Dogecoin), based on its October 31 snapshot. That is considerably fewer than the 55 assets covered in its previous snapshot, with many smaller assets still missing. Once again, this is useful, but as I have explained multiple times, Proof of Reserves are never a comprehensive audit of an exchange's financial condition and stability. More cryptocurrency withdrawals are being restored in phases, although availability still depends on the specific asset and network. Native Dogecoin withdrawals were included in the first reopening phase. The usual reminder: please make sure you select the actual Dogecoin network when withdrawing to your own wallet, rather than stuff like Binance-pegged DOGE on BSC. My recommendation stays the same: withdraw when and if you can, without rushing, and after verifying that you have good backups of your wallets and that the wallet you are using is safe.

As native Dogecoin withdrawals have now been restored, Mishaboar shared his "usual reminder" to the Dogecoin community, telling them to ensure they select the actual Dogecoin network when withdrawing to their wallets, "rather than stuff like Binance-pegged DOGE on BSC," he said.

A Binance-Pegged Dogecoin, a BEP-20 token, runs on the BNB Smart Chain (BSC), and the distinction between it and the actual DOGE coin on the Dogecoin network remains essential to prevent confusion.

Mishaboar also advised users not to rush the withdrawal process, noting that wallet security and proper backup procedures should come first: "My recommendation remains the same: withdraw when and if you can, without rushing, and after verifying that you have good backups of your wallets and that the wallet you are using is secure."

Major crypto exchange Binance recently published initial findings and a response update on the October 29 security incident.

According to Binance, the attackers targeted multiple engineers through malicious software, including an earlier attempt involving a tampered devtool installer and a fake job interview that delivered malware. They then exploited an internal AI tool and stolen deployment credentials to gain access to Binance's wallet-signing infrastructure.

The incident involves assets of some $6.34 billion. About $2.85 billion worth of assets have been intercepted or frozen as of the update.

As of Oct. 31, 2026, Binance said it has restored 98% of the affected token assets and is working on purchasing outstanding, more illiquid assets.

Binance stated that the entry points identified as having been used in the attack have been closed, and it is engaging independent third-party cybersecurity experts to conduct a comprehensive review of the incident.

#5199. 220 Billion Shiba Inu (SHIB): Result of the Biggest Outflow Streak in 2026

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The tide seems to be turning for Shiba Inu (SHIB) on exchanges after a big wave of token withdrawals. Shiba Inu's on-chain data suggests that netflows are finally positive which essentially ends the biggest outflow trend that was fueled by the most recent Binance hack and closed withdrawals.

According to the latest exchange data, around 429 billion SHIB flowed into exchanges, with 276 billion being removed which corresponds to a positive netflow of roughly 153 billion tokens, eliminating the withdrawal trend present before.

Withdrawals are still heavy, though, with the outflow spike of around 220 billion tokens in the last 7 days.

Exchange reserves currently show 85 trillion, which is a normal reading for the token in the last few years. All available SHIB on exchanges at the current price is worth approximately $400 million.

Usually, outflows from exchanges are seen as a sign of accumulation among investors, since they move their tokens into wallets instead of keeping them on exchanges to sell at the best moment possible.

Rising inflows, on the other hand, are considered a negative signal, which could pinpoint an upcoming selling activity, despite the fact that deposits aren't necessarily turning into selling pressure right away.

SHIB looks bearish from a technical perspective, with the daily timeframe showing the token trading near $0.00000465, below its major moving averages. SHIB has also broken below an ascending support trendline.

The $0.0000050–$0.0000052 zone is now the key resistance, and the longer-term moving average near $0.0000055 should be considering the main target for flipping the mid to long-term trend.

The Relative Strength Index (RSI) stays in the lower part of its range. Momentum is weak, but a reversal isn't confirmed until volume turn to adequate levels.

The withdrawal wave has clearly calmed down, but not by the extent needed for a proper market reversal at this point in time.

The 153 billion SHIB netflow we are witnessing now could add even more selling pressure, so it's important to stay cautious if you're a large meme coin holder.

Unfortunately, to gain control back, buyers would need substantial volume injection and a push above $0.000005, until then, SHIB balances will keep growing substantially on exchanges and causing a pressure spike at some point in time in the future.

#5198. Trump Media Moves $344 Million in Bitcoin to Crypto.com as TRUMP Hits a Record Low: How Are Trump’s Crypto Ventures Doing?

TRUMP crypto check: Trump Media just moved its BTC collateral to Crypto.com weeks before a $1B note deadline. Is it selling?

TRUMP crypto ventures came under fresh pressure after Trump Media moved 4,259 BTC, about $344 million, out of wallets holding its convertible-note collateral and into a Crypto.com-linked address. This happened a day after the TRUMP memecoin hit a record low. Lookonchain flagged the transfer at 03:00 UTC. The blockchain shows two transactions, of 2,140.04 BTC and 2,119.31 BTC, confirmed in block 974,613 at 02:17 UTC.

The move lands in a bad week for every Trump-linked asset. After Tuesday’s US midterm elections went the Democrats’ way, the TRUMP token, World Liberty Financial’s WLFI and Trump Media’s stock all fell, even as Bitcoin climbed back above $80,000.

The question traders are asking is simple: are the Trump businesses selling their Bitcoin? The blockchain answers part of it.

Trump Media transferred 4,259 $BTC ($343.67M) to https://t.co/uXlOLXEpBd 40 minutes ago.https://t.co/b8FnEmGpPh pic.twitter.com/A3yEfMmhuf

— Lookonchain (@lookonchain) November 5, 2026

DISCOVER: Bitcoin Back Above $80,000 as $468 Million of Shorts Get Wiped Out: Can BTC Clear Its Pre-Hack High?

The amount is the first clue. Trump Media’s second-quarter 10-Q says 4,260.73 BTC served as collateral for its convertible notes, with restrictions lifted “no later than” May 29, 2028. Thursday’s two transfers add up to 4,259.35 BTC, and both sending wallets are now empty.

The address the coins went to is not new either. The same Crypto.com-linked deposit address, 34jvUbzJ…yMy, took 2,649.9 BTC from Trump Media wallets on May 22 and 2,628.3 BTC on August 1. Each time, the coins were swept into one large wallet that holds about 17,330 BTC from many users.

Once coins sit in a pooled exchange wallet, the blockchain can no longer show whether they were sold, lent or simply held in custody. Trump Media has not commented on Thursday’s move. The timing is what traders are focused on.

A registration statement filed on November 2 says holders of Trump Media’s $1 billion convertible notes can force the company to buy them back for cash on November 30, 2026, and that it “could be required to pay up to approximately $1.0 billion”.

The same filing lists ways to raise the money, including to “sell our bitcoin or other digital asset holdings to fund such repurchases.” At about $80,600, the 4,259 BTC that just moved are worth roughly a third of that bill.

The company’s Bitcoin pile was already shrinking. Trump Media held 9,477.16 BTC on June 30, down from 9,542.16 at the end of 2025, and booked a $360.6 million loss on its digital assets in the first half of the year.

American Bitcoin, the miner co-founded by Eric Trump, is adding coins rather than dumping them. It reported on November 4 that its reserve grew to about 8,427 BTC at the end of September, from 8,002 in June.

It mined about 891 BTC in the quarter but only kept about 425 of them, because it “sold a portion of Bitcoin production to cover operating costs.” That is a miner paying its bills, not a treasury exit.

Almost every listed or tradable Trump business is near its lows, with one exception: the stablecoin.

Token prices are from Binance at 09:00 UTC on Thursday, with peaks and USD1 supply from CoinGecko. Stock prices are Wednesday’s Nasdaq closes.

The TRUMP token took the biggest hit. It fell from $1.396 to a record low of $1.219 in a single hour from 17:00 UTC on Wednesday, and it now trades 98.3% below its $73.43 peak from January 2025.

Trump Media closed at $4.49 on Wednesday, just above its 52-week low of $4.46. Besides the note deadline, it is asking shareholders to approve an all-stock merger with fusion energy company TAE Technologies at a special meeting on December 14.

American Bitcoin’s numbers look better than its stock. It reported third-quarter net income of about $126 million, mostly from a $197 million gain on its Bitcoin, yet the shares are down 26% in a month.

USD1 is the only piece holding steady, trading at its $1 peg with $3.70 billion in circulation.

TRUMP traded at $1.26 on Thursday morning, far below its 20-day EMA at $1.63 and its 50-day EMA at $1.78. It has no earlier trading history below Wednesday’s $1.219 low.

TRUMPUSDT 1D chart. Source: TradingViewTradingView

A bounce in TRUMP would need buyers who see value in Trump-branded assets after the vote. For now, the cheapest signal to watch is the onchain one: if more Trump Media coins head to the same Crypto.com address before November 30, TRUMP crypto sentiment is likely to stay heavy.

#5196. DeFi vault regulation splits Aave, Kamino and Veda from Morpho: Details

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The DeFi industry is split on the best way for the U.S. SEC to regulate popular yield-generating crypto vaults.

On Tuesday, the 3rd of November, Aave, Solana-based Kamino, and crypto vault infrastructure firm Veda wrote to the SEC on how vaults should be treated.

Our argument: securities regulation should follow the rights, obligations, and powers an onchain arrangement actually creates. Who owns the assets? Who promises repayment? Who exercises control?

According to the trio, human discretion or active management by a DAO on crypto vaults should not automatically trigger the SEC’s securities laws. The lending protocols contend that, provided users still own assets and can withdraw them at will, such vaults can be deemed non-custodial.

For Aave, Kamino, and Veda, most vaults sit outside the current SEC’s security definition. This is slightly different from the approach sought by Morpho and the Crypto Council for Innovation (CCI).

Vaults are like managed funds such as mutual funds or ETFs, but on-chain and run by smart contracts. They allow users to have automated yield strategies to make their idle assets productive. Some are managed by risk curators like Steakhouse or Gauntlet, while others are fully run by code.

For the Morpho-backed CCI framework, some vaults run by risk curators could be deemed securities, because they act as managers who pick lending markets. In fact, even DAO-run protocols may partly be viewed as centralized managers and trigger securities law.

However, Aave, Kamino, and Veda disagree with some parts of this argument. For them, DAOs are for “governance, not management.” On the vaults managed by risk curators, Aave added,

A curator paid to make investment decisions about securities may be an investment adviser, but it does not have custody, because the code does not let it take the users’ assets.

That’s part of the user control and asset ownership argument the trio put across. In other words, the DeFi players agree that some of the current crypto vaults may be non-securities. But they split on how human intervention (risk curators and DAOs) impacts how the SEC should treat vaults.

The SEC did acknowledge that not all vaults are the same in a recent guideline. It will be interesting to see how the SEC addresses the human intervention question in vaults.

That said, vaults’ traction increased by 61% in H2, rising from $7B to $11B in assets under management (AUM), and are expected to explode alongside the tokenization boom.

#5195. 'Don’t Be Silly': Satoshi Associate Dismisses Bitcoin Quantum Collapse Claims

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Bitcoin developers have renewed the discussion about the long-term security of the network amid the development of quantum computing ("Q-Day"). The trigger was an interview StarkWare CEO Eli Ben-Sasson gave to journalist Pete Rizzo.

According to Ben-Sasson, without the implementation of a "quantum fix," Bitcoin will lose its leadership, and its market value will collapse due to the physical limitations of current computing systems.

Criticism of these forecasts came from Adam Back, the creator of Hashcash, co-founder of Blockstream and one of the cryptographers mentioned by Satoshi Nakamoto in the Bitcoin whitepaper. On the social network X, Back described the panic expectations with the phrase: "c'mon guys, don't be silly and alarmist."

According to Back, the risks are exaggerated, since algorithms for protection against quantum threats are already at the protocol planning stage. He pointed out that Ben-Sasson's own company, StarkWare, builds its products on STARK technology: zero-knowledge proofs that run on cryptographic hash functions.

In the Bitcoin technical community, hash functions are considered the main security factor. According to theoretical models, quantum computers running Shor's algorithm are capable of breaking the network's current asymmetric cryptography (ECDSA and Schnorr).

However, this method is ineffective against hash functions (including SHA-256). Grover's algorithm gives quantum systems only a quadratic speedup in brute-force search, which, according to Back, is fully neutralized by Bitcoin's standard security settings.

As an alternative scenario, Satoshi Nakamoto's associate cited data on the community's current technical developments, which by late 2026 have moved into the testing phase:

SHRINCS protocol: A modified hash-based signature (Shrunken SPHINCS). According to Blockstream developers, they optimized it for Bitcoin's bandwidth limits.

Implementation status: An official draft Bitcoin Improvement Proposal (BIP) has been published for review. According to the project's roadmap, the technology is undergoing stress tests on the Liquid sidechain.

Critics of post-quantum cryptography often name data volume as the main problem. Quantum-resistant signatures are bulkier than standard 64-byte Schnorr keys, which, according to a number of researchers, could overload the blockchain and raise fees.

Back argues that the infrastructure is capable of scaling through data aggregation. According to his position, batch verification methods and STARK hash proofs make it possible to compress thousands of signatures into one compact package. According to supporters of this approach, the solution will preserve the network's speed and protect its decentralization.

The current discussion demonstrates the existence of two polar views: part of the industry considers the quantum threat an urgent problem, while Bitcoin core developers speak of planned testing of the protective infrastructure, which is proceeding in parallel with the development of quantum capabilities.

#5194. Chainalysis Describes Recovery Efforts After Mercado Bitcoin Security Incident

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Chainalysis said on November 2 that it had supported tracing and containment efforts following a security incident involving Mercado Bitcoin. Incident estimates can differ between early reports, technical responders and the affected platform.

The difference between “identified”, “frozen” and “returned” is easy to lose in fast-moving security coverage. It matters to victims because only the last of those creates a completed recovery. Clear language also protects the credibility of investigators by avoiding a bigger success claim than the evidence supports.

Chainalysis said on November 2 that it had supported tracing and containment efforts following a security incident involving Mercado Bitcoin. The analytics company referred to an approximately $49 million incident and said nearly $10 million had been frozen with help from partners. These figures describe the company’s account of tracing and freezes, not a final audited loss statement or confirmed reimbursement to every customer. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Blockchain analytics can reconstruct movements between addresses and help investigators identify where suspected proceeds arrive. When funds reach a service with intervention powers, the operator may be able to freeze a balance or respond to a legal request. But tracing a token, freezing it and returning its value are three separate operations involving different evidence and legal approvals.

The response illustrates why cryptocurrency incidents increasingly depend on cooperation between issuers, investigators and exchanges. Decentralised settlement can be fast, while preservation of assets may require centralised intervention at a service endpoint. That tension is not new, but it becomes visible when investigators publish how much suspected value they could immobilise.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

Incident estimates can differ between early reports, technical responders and the affected platform. Figures should therefore be attributed and not blended into a supposedly exact final loss. A freeze does not establish the funds can be released immediately, and analysts should not equate linked addresses with a legal finding against every downstream wallet holder.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

Follow official updates for verified loss totals, recovery procedures and whether the affected platform changes its custody or operational controls. For users, the practical questions are whether balances remain accessible, how claims will be handled and what evidence supports any reimbursement promise. A transparent accounting of outcomes is more important than a dramatic seizure headline.

A new blockchain project, Yellow, that previously attracted $10 million in personal investment from Ripple co-founder Chris Larsen has faced massive criticism from members of the XRP community immediately after the listing of its token. Observers’ attention was drawn to asset-selling operations from a wallet that is linked to the developers.

The project’s token was launched simultaneously on the Ethereum and XRP Ledger (XRPL) networks. Due to the absence of a direct technical bridge between the blockchains and high initial demand, the asset’s price on XRPL turned out to be significantly higher than on the Ethereum network.

During this period, tokens worth about 20,000 XRP were sold on the XRPL market from a wallet associated with the startup’s team. Project representative Burak Cem explained in private chats that these transactions were “team wallet arbitraging,” necessary to reduce the emerging price premium and align the rates.

The team’s actions provoked a mixed reaction in the community. Kaj Leroy, a developer popular in the XRP Ledger ecosystem, expressed doubt about the technical possibility of conducting full-fledged arbitrage trades without a working cross-chain bridge.

According to critics, such operations on the first day of trading effectively amount to “liquidity extraction” at the expense of early retail buyers.

The incident developed in the context of a public discussion started by influencer JC Rivera , who reproached the XRP community for systematic skepticism toward new startups. In response, opponents led by Kaj Leroy justified the community’s wariness with the new project’s on-chain data.

@JcRivera @xrplto because they dumped 20k xrp worth on their holders like instantly and said its for arbitrage?🤣 onboarding people to extract this obviously is not the way bro

In their opinion, external teams often use rhetoric about “onboarding” users, while their actions lead to locking in quick profits at the expense of XRP holders’ liquidity.

At the moment, the project, which plans to launch clearing solutions for AI agents, has not provided any additional official comments on the situation. The local community’s level of trust in the new startup remains under pressure.

#5191. Chainalysis Discloses Separate October 30 Security Incident Involving User Data

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Chainalysis disclosed a security incident on October 30 affecting a subset of customer-related information, including names, email addresses and certain user or role labels, according to the company. “No evidence of compromise” is not proof that a particular system was mathematically impossible to access.

The apparent boundary between identity information and investigative data is the central fact in this disclosure. Readers should not dismiss exposed contact details as harmless, but neither should they infer a breach of every product the company operates. Precision is particularly important when the firm itself works in cybercrime investigations.

Chainalysis disclosed a security incident on October 30 affecting a subset of customer-related information, including names, email addresses and certain user or role labels, according to the company. It said there was no evidence that its investigative graph data, credentials or user sessions were compromised. This was a separate incident and should not be merged with Chainalysis’ analysis of the Binance theft. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Businesses that help investigate blockchain crime may also hold conventional personal and organisational information about their customers. Even when a breach does not expose transaction intelligence, names and roles can be useful to attackers crafting phishing or social-engineering messages. That risk depends on the exact data exposed and how those records can be combined with information already public.

The disclosure underscores the value of keeping customer identity systems, investigation data and access credentials in well-defined security boundaries. A malicious actor with one type of information should not automatically gain another. The company’s description can be used to understand the stated scope, but good incident response also includes validating those claims over time.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

“No evidence of compromise” is not proof that a particular system was mathematically impossible to access. It is a statement about what investigators had found as of the company’s update. Affected customers should rely on verified notices and avoid clicking unsolicited reset links. Equally, the existence of a data event does not prove that all users’ credentials were stolen.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

Next, look for the company’s follow-up findings, notice procedures and any confirmed misuse of exposed information. For organisations using investigative vendors, the broader lesson is that sensitive workflows should limit data sharing and use strong independent authentication. Vendor risk exists even when the core analytical product is technically sound.

#5187. ECB Links Europe’s T+1 Securities Transition to a Broader Settlement Overhaul

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: European Central Bank officials have discussed the planned move to T+1 securities settlement in October 2027 alongside longer-term work on tokenized settlement infrastructure. A future regulatory settlement date should not be confused with a finished network rollout.

The deadline matters most to firms that process transactions across several jurisdictions. They have to harmonise instructions, reconcile holdings and manage currencies while the clock gets shorter. Blockchain advocates should explain specifically which tasks become easier and which stay with regulated intermediaries.

European Central Bank officials have discussed the planned move to T+1 securities settlement in October 2027 alongside longer-term work on tokenized settlement infrastructure. A speech published October 29 outlined how shortening the conventional settlement cycle and experimenting with new technology fit into wider financial-market modernisation. The two projects are related strategic topics, not a statement that blockchain technology will automatically replace Europe’s existing settlement systems next year. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

T+1 means most eligible securities trades settle one business day after the trade date, compared with a longer conventional cycle. Shorter cycles can reduce outstanding exposures and certain capital needs, but they compress the time available to resolve breaks, fund positions and coordinate across time zones. Tokenization addresses a different design question: how ownership records and money can move using new technical infrastructure.

The connection is operational discipline. A market trying to settle sooner needs robust automation, accurate records and predictable funding. Tokenized assets may eventually support more tightly connected workflows, but they are not necessary to achieve the near-term T+1 timetable. Existing post-trade institutions and banks still have crucial responsibilities.

A tokenized instrument must connect technical records with enforceable financial rights. Trading software can move a token quickly, but an issuer, custodian, transfer agent or settlement bank may still have to recognise the result. This is why tokenization announcements need to explain which part of the lifecycle is changing, not merely advertise that a blockchain is involved.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: DTCC’s tokenized securities infrastructure rollout and tokenized fund redemptions tested over a weekend. They explain related market structure and should not be mistaken for evidence that every new proposal is already live.

A future regulatory settlement date should not be confused with a finished network rollout. Different products and markets can have exclusions or distinct implementation rules. Nor should a policy speech be read as authorising every private tokenized security to circulate without existing investor-protection requirements.

Real settlement infrastructure is judged by whether it works on bad days, not just during presentations. Reconciliation, asset eligibility, funding, corporate actions and recovery from errors remain central. Continuous technical operation can improve coordination, yet it does not create market liquidity or remove the obligations of regulated participants.

Watch for detailed industry testing, bank funding arrangements and official updates on both T+1 readiness and central-bank-money experiments. The important measure is whether settlement failures and costs fall without shifting uncontrolled risk to participants. Europe’s timeline creates a concrete deadline, while the tokenization debate remains a longer-term architecture choice.

Binance experienced crypto’s largest hack in its history, worth roughly $6.3 billion, last week. The cryptocurrency exchange temporarily paused withdrawals while investigating the attack and updating its security infrastructure. Withdrawals resumed only on Sunday at the start of this month, and even then in phases to avoid a major episode.

The resumption of withdrawals, the release of proof of reserves, the freezing of more than $2 billion in stablecoins, and CZ’s immediate intervention helped mitigate a situation that could have easily spiraled out of control. Binance officials insist that the vast majority of affected assets (roughly 95%) are replenished, with major tokens such as Bitcoin, Ethereum, USDT, BNB, etc. set to have full backing. The exchange’s overall assets are worth around $171 billion, and it remains profitable.

Independent on-chain reporters like DefiLlama and others identified major outflows from the company in the immediate aftermath of the hack. However, the situation eventually changed for Binance, as later reporting put net inflows around $2.3 billion, still less than the $2.8 billion figure touted by CZ.
