#5183. SEC Updates Crypto-Asset FAQs as Firms Seek Clarity on Federal Securities Rules

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: The SEC Division of Corporation Finance added questions 1.4, 1.5 and 1.6 to its crypto-assets FAQ on November 2, 2026. SEC staff positions may be informative without having the legal force of a statute, judicial judgment or final Commission rule.

This distinction is worth making because crypto regulation often arrives in several formats: speeches, staff statements, enforcement actions, proposals and final rules. They have different implications even when the subject is the same. A dated FAQ update merits attention without being promoted into a sweeping legal turning point.

The SEC Division of Corporation Finance added questions 1.4, 1.5 and 1.6 to its crypto-assets FAQ on November 2, 2026. The new staff answers address governance through foundations or organisations, what counts as control over participation and the difference between a functional network and a decentralised one. The page expressly says its answers represent staff views, have no legal force and have not been approved or disapproved by the Commission. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

A functioning crypto system can have a native asset that users need for programmatic activity without necessarily being decentralised. Under the staff explanation, decentralisation is a distinct question about whether any party or group holds operational, economic or voting control. A foundation or association involved in broad-based governance is not automatically a controlling central party; the actual powers and decision rules matter. This is a narrower, more useful clarification than saying every governance token has received a new legal classification.

The additions give lawyers and builders more concrete language for evaluating governance arrangements. A protocol may distribute votes widely while retaining privileged access, unilateral upgrade powers or concentrated economic influence. The staff view asks analysts to study those powers rather than rely on the mere existence of a formal foundation. That could influence how firms document their networks, although it does not eliminate case-by-case analysis or statutory legal requirements.

Crypto policy updates arrive as proposals, staff explanations, enforcement cases and final rules. Those documents carry different legal weight even when they use similar language. A careful article tells readers who issued the text, when it was published and whether it changes obligations now. That distinction is especially important for financial firms making compliance decisions.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: DTCC’s tokenization service and settlement controls and financial infrastructure and technology policy coverage. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

SEC staff positions may be informative without having the legal force of a statute, judicial judgment or final Commission rule. Investors should also resist the leap from regulatory discussion to token-price predictions. A frequently asked questions page is not an approval notice for any specific exchange, security or stablecoin.

Regulation ultimately affects operational choices: where customer assets are held, who can move them, how decisions are recorded and which risks are disclosed. The market can debate policy direction, but firms still need to map any published guidance to existing duties and the specific activity they carry out. General labels rarely answer those questions.

Watch for direct Commission statements, formal rulemaking notices, court decisions and clarifications that identify the affected transactions. Teams reviewing compliance can use the FAQ alongside primary legal sources, while documenting areas where further advice is necessary. Precision about who issued a document is essential to accurate policy reporting.

#5182. Ripple CEO Sees Bipartisan Crypto Support After U.S. Midterm Elections, Renews Call for Clear Rules

Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse has expressed confidence in the crypto industry’s political position after the November 3 U.S. midterm elections, citing victories by pro-crypto candidates from both major parties.

However, despite Democrats securing control of Congress and industry-backed political committees suffering several expensive defeats, Garlinghouse believes efforts to establish clear digital asset regulations in the country remain on track.

In a Wednesday post on X, Garlinghouse highlighted the election victories of lawmakers supportive of the crypto industry, arguing that political backing for digital assets extends beyond party lines.

“Pro-crypto members won on both sides of the aisle….and the 67M (and growing) Americans using crypto aren’t going anywhere. New Congress, with the same mission: clear rules, built in America,” he wrote.

Meanwhile, the industry is assessing the election’s impact on crypto regulation. Despite Democrats gaining control of both chambers, Ripple believes bipartisan support for digital assets will keep efforts to secure clear rules moving forward.

Ripple Chief Legal Officer Stuart Alderoty also weighed in on the election outcome, rejecting suggestions that the results represented a major defeat for the crypto industry.

He highlighted Fairshake’s performance, a crypto-backed political action committee that supported candidates during the election cycle. According to Alderoty, the organization secured meaningful victories in both primary contests and the general election.

He further referenced preliminary figures from the Stand With Crypto initiative, which indicated that a substantial number of pro-crypto lawmakers had secured seats across party lines.

“Let me be clear — some headlines will call this a setback for crypto. The facts are far more nuanced,” Alderoty stated.

He attributed the outcome to years of political engagement involving grassroots supporters, industry organizations and advocacy groups. The CLO believes these efforts have helped establish relationships with lawmakers who could play an important role in shaping future digital asset legislation.

Beyond the election results, the Ripple executive also emphasized the growing number of Americans participating in the crypto economy.

According to Alderoty, more than 67 million Americans use digital assets for activities ranging from building financial independence to running businesses and making payments.

Additionally, banks and businesses are increasingly adopting crypto-related services, reflecting the industry’s growing presence in mainstream finance.

For Ripple, this growing adoption reinforces the need for legislation that gives businesses and consumers a clearer understanding of the rules governing digital assets.

Politically, the election results were mixed for the crypto industry, particularly for organizations that spent heavily supporting pro-crypto candidates.

According to Reuters, Fairshake-backed candidates had won 29 of the 34 races decided as of its November 4 report. The committee supported 32 House candidates, including 19 Republicans and 13 Democrats.

However, the industry also suffered notable defeats despite its considerable campaign spending.

One of the biggest setbacks came in Ohio, where Democrat Sherrod Brown won a Senate seat despite crypto-backed political committees spending more than $60 million to defeat him.

Overall, crypto interests spent about $200 million during the election cycle, highlighting the industry’s growing involvement in U.S. politics and its determination to influence the direction of digital asset regulation.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Stablecoin adoption continues to grow on various blockchain platforms. Against that growth, TRON appears to be the most widely held platform by the number of stablecoin holders.

In Q3 2026, TRON had an average of 76.53 million stablecoin holders. Therefore, it accounts for approximately 40.45% of all the stablecoin holders on the 45 blockchain platforms that were monitored.

These figures indicate that TRON [TRX] was significantly ahead of Ethereum [ETH]  with an average of 26.32 million stablecoin holders.

These results reflect the increasing use of stablecoins as a medium of exchange for making transfers and payments and a reflection of the increased demand to move around digital money.

Also, the 30-day average of TRON has been above 79 million in Q4, indicating continued support for the platform. This growing user base could attract more stablecoin activity, deepen network liquidity, and reinforce TRON’s position in digital payments.

While TRON leads stablecoin adoption by holder numbers, Ripple USD [RLUSD] is gaining ground through rising supply and inflows. Notably, the current circulation supply for RLUSD has reached over $2.5 billion.

The rise in stablecoin holders and RLUSD supply now raises a bigger question: are users actively moving these assets across networks? While TRON shows actual use of these assets, it appears that Tether [USDT] accounts nearly 98% of TRON’s stablecoin supply, standing at $94.3 billion.

This concentration supports heavy payment and transfer activity, reflected in 3.9 million daily active addresses and over 12 million transactions. The network also generates roughly $1.1 million in daily fees, showing the economic value of this activity.

TRON [TRX] leads stablecoin adoption, with 76.53 million holders and strong USDT activity.

Ripple USD [RLUSD] has surpassed $2.5 billion in supply, but XRPL usage remains weaker.

#5180. Apple Joins 59-Company Strike to Freeze U.S. Stock Tokenization

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Apple has officially blocked the launch of tokenized trading in its shares on the new American platform OKXICE TSV. Exercising its thirty-day veto right under the SEC's "innovation exception," the iPhone manufacturer supported a large-scale demarche by issuers.

By November 2026, the number of boycotting companies had reached 59, which effectively paralyzed the largest regulatory experiment on crossing Wall Street with the crypto market.

🚨NEW: Three more issuers have objected to @okx/@ICE_Markets tokenizing their shares, bringing the total to 59: Apple The Home Depot GE Vernova Six of the Magnificent Seven have now objected. Meta wasn’t on the original list.

The OKXICE TSV project — a joint venture between the crypto exchange OKX and Intercontinental Exchange, the owner of the NYSE — was preparing to launch round-the-clock on-chain trading through Uniswap v4 pools on the XLayer blockchain.

However, at the moment, the listing has been blocked by six of the seven members of the "Magnificent Seven" (all except Meta, which was not initially on the lists), as well as SpaceX, The Home Depot, GE Vernova, JPMorgan Chase and Goldman Sachs.

Insiders link this coordinated refusal to corporations' unwillingness to lose control over capital and to harsh behind-the-scenes pressure from the traditional exchange Nasdaq, which actively persuaded issuers to derail the initiative.

This internal boycott has exposed a deep regulatory gap: while the legal union of TradFi and DeFi on U.S. territory has been put on pause by the issuers themselves, international capital has long been trading digital analogs of U.S. securities outside the country.

By the end of 2026, when global Bitcoin ownership reached 4.5–6% of the planet's population, an autonomous blockchain liquidity had formed in offshore jurisdictions.

On foreign venues such as Kraken, Bybit and the international circuit of OKX, third-party versions of Apple shares are actively circulating, strictly closed to American residents. Among them, the leader in liquidity is the AAPLx token from Backed Finance on Solana and Ethereum with 1:1 custodial backing of shares, the tokenized AAPLon product from Ondo with automatic dividend reinvestment, as well as the new AAPL tokens from Backpack, subject to direct exchange for physical shares.

Corporate America, in the person of Apple, is defending its sovereignty in the home market, but the offshore market will not be stopped by this.

#5179. Litecoin Takes Savage Swipe at XRP With Controversial Post That Has Crypto Community Talking— Here’s What Happened

Litecoin has reignited its long-running rivalry with XRP after taking another provocative jab at the Ripple-linked cryptocurrency in a post that quickly caught the crypto community’s attention. This time, the Litecoin account joked that it could no longer use a controversial term when referring to XRP, prompting fresh reactions from the XRPArmy. The remark has added another twist to the ongoing rivalry between the two crypto communities.

In the Nov. 5 post, Litecoin wrote: “Me finding out I can’t use the word retarded for XRP anymore.” The remark seemed to poke fun at XRP while referencing restrictions on the offensive term, adding another provocative moment to the rivalry between the two crypto communities.

One XRP supporter was quick to fire back, questioning Litecoin’s standing in the crypto market and accusing it of underperforming against other established cryptocurrencies for years. The user argued that Litecoin had little room to mock XRP while Zcash had moved far ahead of it

The XRP supporter’s criticism also reflects a notable difference in the two assets’ recent market performance. Zcash (ZEC) has surged approximately 138% in 2026, while Litecoin (LTC) has fallen around 30% year-to-date, according to comparative market data. Litecoin has also lagged behind XRP in annualized returns over the past five years, giving the supporter’s argument some market context.

Another XRP supporter joined the discussion, questioning Litecoin’s relevance and taking a swipe at its real-world adoption, asking: “Has anyone used Litecoin in the past 10 years?”

The latest jab is not the first time Litecoin has taken aim at XRP and its community. The rivalry became particularly heated in August 2025, when Litecoin’s official X account published a lengthy, sarcastic post disguised as a “fun fact.” The message mocked XRP’s ambitions in the banking sector and ridiculed the narrative that Ripple’s payment infrastructure could transform traditional finance.

The post also took a personal swipe at Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse, calling him “Brad Garlicmouse” in a satirical remark. It further questioned the value of XRP’s banking-related use cases and drew criticism from members of the XRP community.

The latest back-and-forth comes as both cryptocurrencies continue to trade significantly below their previous all-time highs. Litecoin is hovering around $55.57, approximately 86.5% below its 2021 peak, while XRP trades at $1.31 after losing roughly 64% of its value since hitting its $3.65 all-time high. Despite the sharp corrections, both assets remain in the spotlight as their communities debate their market performance and long-term relevance.

#5178. Solana Microscope Offers Open-Source Monitoring for Complex On-Chain Operations

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: The Solana Foundation has described Microscope, an open-source monitoring approach for teams managing on-chain programmes and infrastructure. An alert is not a transaction blocker.

Operational tooling rarely generates the excitement of a new token launch, yet it can determine whether institutions trust a network enough to hold meaningful value on it. Teams want evidence that they can detect unexpected actions before the problem expands. Monitoring is one part of that process, not a substitute for preventive controls.

The Solana Foundation has described Microscope, an open-source monitoring approach for teams managing on-chain programmes and infrastructure. The toolkit is designed to help operators inspect instructions, transactions and alerts while connecting observations to common monitoring workflows. It is an engineering tool, not an announcement that every Solana application now receives automatic protection against attacks. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

In a complex blockchain application, important activity can be hidden within technical transaction instructions that a general-purpose dashboard does not explain well. Monitoring tools try to decode those events and route relevant signals to people who can respond. This is useful for treasury movements, contract administration and unusual operational behaviour, but good alerts depend on choosing the right thresholds and maintaining the integrations.

The wider lesson is that on-chain transparency is not the same thing as operational comprehension. Transaction data may be public while a business still struggles to identify why an event matters or who should investigate it. A self-hostable tool can give teams more control over their monitoring stack, provided they manage uptime, access and alert delivery responsibly.

There is a meaningful gap between a fast transaction and a complete financial service. Solana infrastructure can settle digital assets, but an actual payments or securities product must also manage eligibility, legal rights, local currencies and exceptions. The best reporting follows the full workflow rather than assuming the chain’s speed is the entire product.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on Solana’s Samsung Wallet rollout and user test and the latest Solana developer and payments coverage. They explain related market structure and should not be mistaken for evidence that every new proposal is already live.

An alert is not a transaction blocker. Microscope does not automatically fix a vulnerable programme, reverse an executed transfer or eliminate the need for security audits. False alarms can consume attention, while incomplete alert definitions can miss harmful activity. A monitored environment still requires tested incident procedures and clear accountability.

Distribution can be an advantage for blockchain applications, but the user experience needs to justify a change in behaviour. Developers also need monitoring and controls to support systems that operate continuously. A credible launch is one that describes available features, supported participants and how success will be measured after the initial announcement.

Watch for adoption in real applications, public documentation and integration examples showing how teams respond to alerts. The most useful evidence would involve measured detection speed and fewer unresolved incidents, rather than the number of dashboards deployed. For Solana developers, monitoring belongs alongside code review and permission design as a basic operating requirement.

#5177. CASHCAT rallies 11%: Will bullish bets push the memecoin towards $0.12?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The Cash Cat [CASHCAT] memecoin recorded one of the notable rebounds on the 3rd of November, at a time when the broader crypto market remained in a somewhat neutral but bullish phase, with market capitalization hitting $2.61 trillion.

The asset is up 11% in the past 24 hours, driven by growing interest in the perpetual market and broader capital flows, which suggest memecoin traders may be preparing for another mini-rally. However, the path towards a rally is not a clear one, as obstacles still lie ahead.

The surge in Cash Cat’s price has perpetual traders at its center. CoinGlass showed that the Funding Rate surged significantly, reaching a high of 0.0432% as of November 4, indicating that traders are opening more long positions than shorts.

This situation occurs when traders holding long positions pay the periodic funding fee as the price moves in their direction.

During this period, Open Interest, which measures the capital in the perpetual market, has also expanded, reaching $50.77 million. Roughly 18%, or $9.23 million, of that inflow has come in the past 24 hours.

In simple terms, the significant capital inflows and positive Funding Rate suggest that traders are directing fresh capital into long positions to support the rally.

Trading volume across the perpetual market has reflected the growing bullish sentiment.

Perpetual trading volume, tracked through the Long/Short Ratio, shows that over 51% of the total trading volume in the past 24 hours came from long positions, implying more buying activity.

Volume analysis showed that buying activity exceeded 50% across major cryptocurrency exchanges, including Bybit, Gate, and Hyperliquid.

This is important to note, especially when the buying volume comes from exchanges that control a significant portion of the market’s Open Interest, meaning traders on these platforms are likely concentrating their capital on long positions.

Chart analysis points towards the market seeing a significant momentum push, with the Money Flow Index (MFI) and the Bull Bear Power (BBP) indicating this development.

The Money Flow Index crossed above the 50 threshold while maintaining a level of 56.7 over the past day, suggesting that the market is now in a more bullish and stable phase. This phase brings more capital into CASHCAT, further strengthening its existing momentum.

In addition to this, the Bull Bear Power (BBP) indicator has formed two consecutive green bars on the chart, showing that investors are bullish. Yet, given that both histogram bars formed on the 3rd and 4th of November are moderate in size, this reflects that prices are not completely bullish yet.

Chart pattern analysis shows that if bulls maintain their strength, the price is likely to face an obstacle at the $0.122 level, which could significantly weigh on the momentum gathered so far.

Cash Cat surges as its Funding Rate climbed to 0.0432% and Open Interest reached $50.77 million.

Bullish momentum is building, but the $0.122 price level remains a major resistance that could limit further gains.

#5176. Circle's USDC Dominates Stablecoin Market With 79% Share of Transaction Volume

Circle revealed that USDC accounted for 79% of the transaction volume of all stablecoins in the third quarter of 2026.

According to CEO Jeremy Allaire, the CEO of Circle explained that USDC's market share of the transaction volume of all stablecoins increased significantly from 66% in the same period a year ago. The overall USDC on-chain transaction volume was $13.9 trillion, an increase of 49% year-on-year.

This shows the rapid adoption of USDC in payments, trading, and blockchain-based financial applications despite stiff competition in the stablecoin space.

Circle stated that the issuance and redemption volumes of USDC in the third quarter reached a new high. In total, $208 billion of USDC was issued and redeemed in Q3. This translates to an average daily amount of $2.3 billion. For the third quarter, Circle said that the average USDC in circulation was $73.3 billion, which was up 18% year-on-year. This information was part of Circle's Q3 highlights.

Apart from USDC itself, Circle emphasized the growth in other aspects of its infrastructure. Circle indicated that Arc Mainnet went live in the third quarter. Over 100 institutions and applications are already building on Arc. As of Oct. 30, 2026, assets on Arc totaled $1.5 billion, as stated by Circle.

Circle Payment Network, or CPN, was another part of Circle's infrastructure that witnessed rapid growth. The company indicated that CPN ended the third quarter with an annualized payment volume of $41.3 billion. This was 15.2 times higher than in the same period last year, based on activity over the last 30 days. Allaire explained that CPN's payment volume is now more than $58 billion.

Circle also revealed that USDC has now become the leading settlement asset in agentic/automated internet payments. Allaire disclosed that over 99% of agent payments on x402 are settled in USDC.

In the third quarter, Circle's total revenue and reserve income were $865 million, up 17% year-on-year. This figure included $182 million from ARC token sales, according to an image showing Circle's Q3 highlights.

According to Allaire, the impressive performance in the third quarter is a strong signal that Circle is ready for the next phase of stablecoin adoption. He said the GENIUS Act, which is expected to take effect in January, will bring regulated digital dollars into the mainstream. Also, Circle has now activated all the layers of its platform.

The impressive third-quarter data indicates that Circle has not only further solidified USDC's dominance in terms of transaction activity but is also expanding its ecosystem across payments, on-chain finance, and institutional adoption.

#5174. Bitcoin rebounds toward $79K – Can BTC keep climbing without US buyers?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Bitcoin’s [BTC] latest recovery is struggling to attract stronger participation from U.S. buyers, leaving an important source of market demand largely absent.

The Coinbase premium index measures the difference between the price on Coinbase and Binance.

As such, its extended period of time being in the negative direction clearly illustrates that the U.S. has continued to exert selling pressure against the amount of demand from U.S. buyers.

Notably, the index did show a negative reading even at a point when Bitcoin was rising toward $87,000 in September. This implies that there were other regions of the world supporting price appreciation at that time.

If this trend persists, it could cap the size of Bitcoin’s next advance. As a result, it could limit recovery strength and expose Bitcoin to further selling. Conversely, a sustained move above zero would indicate that U.S. demand is beginning to return.

Meanwhile, the combined spot and futures demand indicator for Bitcoin over a 30-day window is currently reporting a decline toward a reading of -200,000 BTC. These results reflect continued net outflows from the global market.

However, this week’s reading has shown some improvements as spot demand has become increasingly less negative. Therefore, these results indicate that sellers appear to be slowing down their sales momentum.

This disparity explains why it continues to be difficult for Bitcoin to maintain its current recovery amid easing downside pressure. On the other hand, declining negative demand could provide stability in terms of maintaining current prices around $77,000.

Ultimately, a sustained return to positive total demand for Bitcoin would significantly improve the prospects for a successful recovery. In particular, if spot buying resumes.

Although Bitcoin demand shows early signs of stabilization, its price structure still reflects considerable selling pressure. BTC has rebounded from $76,000 to around $78,490, but the recovery remains gradual.

This suggests short-term recovery and reduced selling rather than strong new buying. The rebound now faces a critical test near $79,000, where previous support has turned into resistance.

Closing prices above $79,200 could lead to higher prices such as $80,400 and $82,000. On the other hand, if prices are rejected at this level, BTC may decline toward $76,975 and possibly lower toward $76,000.

Overall, Bitcoin must regain the $79,200 price level in order to fortify its current recovery. Failure to do so could result in continued control of the broader downtrend.

Bitcoin [BTC] faces weak U.S. demand despite signs of easing selling pressure.

BTC must reclaim $79,200 to strengthen its recovery, with rejection risking a drop toward $76,000.

#5173. Riot Liquidates 79% of Its Bitcoin Treasury for $28 Billion AI Infrastructure Deal

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

American Riot Platforms Inc. (RIOT) sold off 79% of its Bitcoin in the third quarter of 2026, redirecting capital toward building infrastructure for artificial intelligence. According to a fresh financial report, the company reduced its cryptocurrency reserves from 11,380 to 2,418 BTC.

In total, over the first nine months of the year, Riot sold 20,264 BTC on the market, receiving about $1.5 billion for them. This money went toward financing a major technological shift: the company's free energy capacity is being repurposed for high-performance computing needs.

Riot shares reacted to the session open with a sharp jump to $16.20, after which they stabilized in the $15.45–$15.50 range, holding positions on elevated volumes.

The main incentive for selling the cryptocurrency was a 15-year agreement to lease ready-made sites and capacities (powered land lease) totaling 910 MW at the Corsicana campus in Texas. The strategic partner was the Frontier AI Lab (Anthropic) laboratory.

The initial cost of the contract is estimated at $28 billion, and the average annual rent at the start will be about $1.8 billion. Taking into account other projects, Riot's total long-term revenue in the data center segment has reached $38 billion, and the total controlled capacity is 1,151 MW.

Riot CEO Jason Les noted that the agreement is structured as a triple-net lease. Under its terms, the client itself purchases and maintains the computing equipment. For Riot, this reduces construction costs by roughly half compared to standard Tier 3 data centers. As a result, the company will be able to do without issuing new shares and diluting investors' stakes.

The shift in focus is occurring against the backdrop of falling margins in classic mining. Riot's quarterly revenue from cryptocurrency mining fell by 28% — to $116 million, although the company actually mined 1,637 BTC.

Nevertheless, total quarterly revenue held at $181.4 million (+1% year-over-year), outpacing market forecasts. Net profit amounted to $4.03 million.

To optimize its balance sheet, the company fully closed its $200 million credit line with Coinbase, returning 5,821 BTC from collateral. Riot ended the quarter with free liquidity of $950.2 million (including cash and the remainder of the cryptocurrency). The balance sheet retains 2,418 BTC, which may also go under the hammer in the fourth quarter to finance the Texas construction.

#5172. Cardano Eyes 2,000% Replay From Current Base

Home » Crypto Market News » Cardano Eyes 2,000% Replay From Current Base

As 131 million ADA tokens sit idle in retired pools, the weekly chart structure points to $1.37 as next-cycle destination.

Cardano is trading near $0.19 on a weekly chart that one analyst compares directly to the base that preceded a roughly 2,000% rally in the last cycle. At the same time, on-chain data shows 131.63 million ADA remains tied to retired stake pools, leaving tens of thousands of holders without rewards until they redelegate.

360Trader highlighted the weekly structure and noted that ADA remains well below the level where a true bull-market stage would begin. That stage, in the analyst’s view, only starts once price clears $1.37.

The more immediate requirement is a reclaim of $0.25. Until then the chart is still forming the same kind of low base that previously launched a 2,000% advance once the broader altcoin market turned. The comparison is structural rather than a near-term price target.

Chart Request winner: $ADA 📊Weekly chart, and it's still well under its bull-market stage. That stage really doesn't start until $1.37 breaks.First it needs to get back above $0.25 quickly before I'd be interested.Last cycle ADA went 2000% off a base just like this one.… pic.twitter.com/4BPAusTmTY

From current levels a move to the prior cycle high would still require a multi-hundred-percent gain, and the larger $1.37 threshold sits even further away.

ADA has not yet reclaimed $0.25, the level 360Trader said would be needed before the setup becomes interesting again. The token has been consolidating near the lower end of its multi-year range while the wider crypto market digests recent pressure.

The analyst’s point is that the base itself is not the signal. Confirmation only arrives when price begins to push out of the accumulation zone and the broader altcoin cycle starts to expand.

Until those conditions appear, the historical 2,000% precedent remains a reminder of what has happened from comparable structures rather than a forecast for the next few weeks.

In related Cardano news, Cexplorer data flagged a practical on-chain issue. Roughly 59,025 delegators still have funds assigned to 2,720 retired stake pools, totaling 131.63 million ADA.

Those pools no longer produce blocks or distribute rewards. Holders retain full control of their tokens and can move the delegation to an active pool at any time. The funds are not locked or lost, but every epoch spent on a retired pool is simply a missed reward.

Cardano Has a 131M ADA Staking ProblemThousands of Cardano (@Cardano) holders may need to check where their $ADA is delegated.Cexplorer reports that 59,025 delegators still have funds assigned to retired stake pools. The total comes to 131.63 million ADA.The explorer… pic.twitter.com/cdTn46aFLr

Community responses noted that checking a pool’s status on an explorer and redelegating takes only a minute and restores eligibility for the next epoch’s rewards.

The technical observation and the staking data sit side by side. One points to a historical base that once produced a 2,000% move once conditions aligned.

The other shows that a measurable portion of the staked supply is currently idle because pool operators retired and holders have not yet updated their delegations.

ADA remains near $0.19, below the $0.25 reclaim level and far from the $1.37 threshold that would mark a shift into a broader bull-market phase.

Whether the base develops the same way as last cycle depends on price reclaiming those levels and on the wider altcoin market providing the liquidity. In the meantime, any Cardano holder still delegated to a retired pool can restore rewards by simply choosing an active one.

#5171. Bitbase launches ORIGIN, distributing 1M points every week

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Panama City, November 4, 2026 — Bitbase today launched ORIGIN, the first season of its new Bitbase Points program. Ahead of the launch, the digital asset exchange credited points to eligible users who had been trading on the platform, so their past activity counts from day one. The season runs for 26 weeks, until May 4, 2027, with 1,000,000 points distributed each week.

ORIGIN is also the first part of Bitbase 2.0 to reach users directly. Bitbase unveiled 2.0 on November 2, with an upgraded app and website and a new brand identity built around the statement “Market Flows. We Focus.” The idea is that markets will always move, and Bitbase chooses to focus on what lasts for its users. ORIGIN applies the same thinking to participation. Rather than rewarding a single burst of activity, it looks at what users do day after day over six months and credits the contribution they make across that period.

Bitbase built ORIGIN around what users already do on the exchange. Eligible users, new or existing, are enrolled automatically, with no separate registration. Points are allocated daily based on each participant’s contribution, which draws on four sources: trading, assets held on Bitbase, consistent activity and referrals. Because consistency is one of those sources, steady participation adds up over the course of the season.

ORIGIN also rewards users who bring others to Bitbase. A referrer receives an additional 25% of the contribution of each new user they refer directly, while the new user’s own contribution is unaffected.

Starting November 9, participants will also receive a shareable Trading Identity card that reflects their trading habits, with a new card issued every Monday until the season ends.

“Bitbase 2.0 is about focus, and for our users, that means knowing their steady participation is recognized and credited,” said Nicholas Lo, Chief Business Officer at Bitbase. “That’s why ORIGIN started by crediting what users had already done with us. It doesn’t ask anyone to trade differently. It simply records the participation that’s already there, every day, for the next six months.”

Users can track their points and rankings in the Bitbase app and on the website. Full details are available at bitbase.com/points.

Bitbase is a digital asset exchange serving individual and institutional users worldwide, with products spanning Spot, Futures, TradFi and Earn. Bitbase aims to give users a stable and straightforward trading experience, in line with its brand statement, “Market Flows. We Focus.” Learn more at bitbase.com.

#5170. XRP Ledger Activates New Amendment to Strengthen Network

The XRP Ledger (XRPL) network recently activated a new amendment that helps secure the network by resolving potential security weaknesses and making some existing functionalities more robust and reliable.

This latest amendment, called fixCleanup3_4_0, was activated on Nov. 4, bringing a series of technical updates that include fixes for token issuance, automated market makers (AMMs), lending functionalities, and other parts of the XRP Ledger network.

A fix for a potential security weak point related to token issuance on the XRP Ledger network is a critical aspect of the latest amendment.

As explained in the official documentation of the XRP Ledger network, this update addresses a potential weakness related to delegated permissions that could allow some accounts to issue tokens when they are only allowed to destroy them.

On the XRP Ledger, token issuers can grant permissions to other accounts to perform certain tasks. For example, a stablecoin issuer could delegate permission to burn tokens to another account when customers want to redeem them.

With the latest amendment, the XRP Ledger network addresses this security weakness by preventing a zero-balance, burn-only delegated payment from resulting in the issuance of new tokens.

On top of addressing token issuance permissions, there's more to the fixCleanup3_4_0 amendment.

As explained in the official XRP Ledger network documentation, the fixCleanup3_4_0 update brings several fixes to decentralized trading, asset management, and transaction processing.

For automated market makers (AMMs), this latest update prevents some invalid auction bids and makes some improvements to how liquidity withdrawals and token clawbacks work.

It also brings some fixes for potential unexpected calculation errors or rounding issues that could occur in some cases.

These changes are supposed to make things more predictable when it comes to liquidity pools and avoid potential failures that could happen in some cases.

Another significant aspect of these changes concerns Single Asset Vaults and the proposed native lending infrastructure on the XRP Ledger.

As explained, this amendment brings some improvements to how rounding is handled when assets enter or exit vaults.

The idea is to ensure that transactions do not result in more or fewer assets being transferred than they should.

#5169. OpenAI Wants to Rebrand AI Data Centers, Use Them to 'Protect the Grid'

OpenAI Wants to Rebrand AI Data Centers, Use Them to 'Protect the Grid'

OpenAI wants its massive AI data centers to help keep the lights on when electricity grids come under pressure. And, apparently, it would also prefer that people stop calling them AI data centers.

The ChatGPT developer announced its Grid Protectors Program on Monday, a national initiative designed to let future AI facilities reduce electricity consumption during emergencies and periods of high demand, potentially lowering costs for other customers.

Under the plan, OpenAI would coordinate with utility companies to determine how much electricity its facilities could temporarily stop using, how quickly they could respond, and for how long. The company is also exploring on-site batteries and power generation to help manage demand.

“Families and small businesses should not be asked to subsidize a compute campus’s electricity needs,” OpenAI said in its announcement.

The program addresses one of the biggest complaints surrounding the AI infrastructure boom: companies are building increasingly power-hungry facilities while residents worry about paying higher—and water—electricity bills to support them.

OpenAI is using a planned facility in Effingham County, Georgia, as its first example. The company has signed a 25-year agreement with Georgia Power covering approximately 3,200 megawatts of electricity demand, with up to 1,000 megawatts available for temporary reductions during periods of high demand.

Under the approved agreement, OpenAI must cover the full infrastructure costs required to serve its facility, rather than passing those expenses on to existing customers.

Georgia Power also projects approximately $950 million in annual customer savings beginning in 2029, equivalent to at least $15 per month for a typical residential customer. However, those estimates cover the utility's broader portfolio of large electricity customers and expected growth, not just OpenAI's project.

The Georgia facility is still under development, and the savings remain projections. OpenAI has not yet disclosed binding demand-reduction commitments for other facilities under its national program.

OpenAI's electricity initiative also arrives after reports of a massive PR campaign to change what people call the facilities in the first place to erase from collective memory the negative sentiment towards AI data centers.

According to Axios, OpenAI is preparing a post-midterm policy and PR campaign that includes rebranding data centers as "AI compute campuses," an effort to make the infrastructure sound more appealing to communities, effectively trying to solve a problem by changing the problem’s name.

The company has already started using the terminology. Its Grid Protectors announcement repeatedly refers to future facilities as "compute campuses," although it occasionally uses the more familiar term "data centers."

An OpenAI spokesperson confirmed the naming strategy to Business Insider, arguing that the term better reflects the facilities' broader role in computing, energy infrastructure, and skilled employment.

The reported rebranding comes as opposition to AI infrastructure projects has become a political issue across the United States. Pennsylvania introduced new restrictions on large data centers in August, including provisions designed to protect residents from higher electricity costs. Other states are following.

OpenAI and six other major technology companies also signed a White House pledge in March to pay for the electricity and grid upgrades their AI projects require.

The new program goes further by proposing that AI facilities could become useful to utilities rather than simply adding pressure to their systems. But the national framework is still largely an ambition, with individual agreements to be negotiated.

OpenAI's Georgia project will provide an early test of that approach. Electricity delivery to the planned 3.2-gigawatt facility is scheduled to begin in 2028 and expand in phases through 2032.

#5168. BitMart founder confirms second exchange hack – Links to Mercado Bitcoin emerge

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Two exchange security incidents and a potential shared threat actor.

November has started with volatility. Macro FUD is driving uncertainty in the market. However, crypto security is also playing its part.

Binance’s exploit of a $6.3 billion hack triggered fresh panic, and now Mercado Bitcoin’s $38 million-$49 million exploit has added another layer of FUD over the past 24 hours.

The key takeaway? Another large-scale hack has occurred, which was confirmed by BitMart founder Sheldon Xia via his recent post on X. Initial investigation shows there may be a link between this and the attackers who exploited the $38M-$49M exploit on Mercado Bitcoin.

In essence, two potentially connected hacks have emerged within just 24 hours.

On-chain investigators are currently tracking down where the funds went. Interestingly on-chain data shows that around $11.45 million worth of altcoins has been moved between various wallets, raising concerns about potential liquidation.

This includes around $1.1 million worth of VIRTUAL being sent to a new wallet, with sales potentially taking place in batches of around $20,000. The attacker still holds around $1 million of VIRTUAL.

Timing is also important here. There is already a lot of crypto market FUD ahead of the US midterm elections. The Altcoin Season Index has also fallen below its third lower low, which means things are not looking great for altcoins.

With exchange hacks adding to the uncertainty, could rising sell pressure trigger a broader altcoin crash?

Altcoins face another test as the Mercado Bitcoin and BitMart hacks trigger fresh market concern.

The MercadoBitcoin hacker has moved to sell multiple altcoins such as PEPE, AAVE, UNI, ONDO, and ENA, converting them into ETH. The attacker now holds 7,988 ETH worth around $18.19 million.

Meanwhile, Arkham Intelligence reports that the hacker has moved 31 different altcoins to wallet 0x848 and sold them for around $316,000 in ETH. In essence, the hacker is rotating out of altcoins and moving into Ethereum.

As the largest altcoin by market cap, Ethereum is now in focus. From a technical perspective, Ethereum [ETH] is trading above $2.3k, and RSI remains in neutral territory. On the ETF side, however, flows have been weak, with Spot ETH ETFs recording seven consecutive days of net outflows.

This indicates that recent ETH strength has less to do with strong demand for ETH and more to do with Bitcoin weakness, with ETH dominance (ETH.D) up nearly 2% this week.

However, the bigger risk is what happens next.

If the hacker starts dumping his $18.19 million worth of ETH, Ethereum could get hit by negative selling pressure and push the sentiment lower.

With the altcoins already struggling, consecutive lower lows, weak ETF flows, and rising macro uncertainty, the market could face another liquidity squeeze, increasing the risk of a deeper altcoin crash.

Two linked exchange hacks have raised fresh fears, with hackers selling altcoins and moving millions into ETH.

Weak market sentiment, ETF outflows, and rising uncertainty could trigger more selling and a wider altcoin crash.

#5167. Bitbase Launches ORIGIN, a Points Season That Rewards Consistency

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

PANAMA CITY, November 4, 2026 — Bitbase today launched ORIGIN, the first season of its Bitbase Points program, which is designed to reward sustained participation over time. Over the 26-week season, which ends on May 4, 2027, Bitbase will distribute 1,000,000 points each week, allocated daily according to each participant’s contribution.

Each participant’s contribution is drawn from four sources: trading, assets held on Bitbase, consistent activity and referrals. Consistent activity is counted as a separate source, so continued participation from day to day adds to a user’s contribution. All eligible users, whether new to Bitbase or already active on the platform, are enrolled automatically.

For each new user referred directly, the referrer receives an additional 25% of that user’s contribution. The new user’s own points are not affected.

Participants will also receive a Trading Identity card, a shareable summary of their trading style. The first cards will be issued on November 9, with new cards following each Monday for the remainder of the season.

Before the season began, Bitbase credited points to eligible existing users based on their earlier activity on the platform. As a result, these users enter ORIGIN with their prior activity already reflected in their points and ranking.

On November 2, the exchange introduced Bitbase 2.0, updating its brand, app and website under the statement “Market Flows. We Focus.” ORIGIN is the first program to put that statement into practice. While markets move continuously, Bitbase has chosen to assess participation across months rather than individual moments.

“We designed ORIGIN for users who participate consistently, not only during the busiest periods in the market,” said Nicholas Lo, Chief Business Officer at Bitbase. “Over a six-month season, that consistency carries weight. Our responsibility is to make sure every contribution is credited, from the first day of the season to the last.”

Points and rankings are available in the Bitbase app and at bitbase.com/points.

#5166. Bitcoin Reclaims $80K as Democrats’ Victory Lifts Markets. Could This Be a Bull Trap?

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Bitcoin Reclaims $80K as Democrats’ Victory Lifts Markets. Could This Be a Bull Trap?

Bitcoin rallied as Democrats took Congress, yet traders worry the move might be a temporary pump.

The Bitcoin price jumped 3.7% to $80,570 after Democrats won control of both chambers of Congress in the November 4 US midterm elections. Optimism returned to financial markets as investors priced in lower political uncertainty, while falling oil and a weaker dollar boosted risk appetite across both stock and crypto markets.

However, despite improving market sentiment, a significant wave of short liquidations helped accelerate Bitcoin’s recovery, raising questions about whether the move reflects genuine buying demand or is simply the result of a temporary short squeeze.

The November 3 US midterm elections saw Democrats win control of both chambers of Congress, prompting investors to reassess the political outlook and the risks surrounding US policy.

Oil prices dropped sharply. Brent crude fell more than 8% as markets priced in the possibility of easing tensions surrounding Donald Trump’s Iran war.

Lower oil prices, a weaker US dollar, and easing geopolitical concerns boosted sentiment across financial markets. The Dow Jones rose 3.03%, its best post-midterm day in 44 years. The S&P 500 gained 1.58%, and the Nasdaq added 1.44%.

Crypto followed the broader market recovery. The total crypto market capitalization rose 2.7% over 24 hours to $2.78 trillion, according to CoinGecko. Ethereum gained 6% to $2,420, while Solana climbed 3.8% to $96.07.

Derivatives market activity may have played a significant role in Bitcoin’s latest move.

Roughly $778.93 million in crypto positions were liquidated in 24 hours, per Coinglass data. Short positions accounted for $491 million, including $273.12 million in Bitcoin shorts.

Institutional investment flows also showed signs of improvement. US spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $74.57 million in net inflows yesterday, ending three days of outflows. BlackRock’s IBIT was the only fund with net outflows, while Fidelity’s FBTC led inflows.

Falling geopolitical and inflation risks often push investors back into riskier assets. When short sellers close losing positions, they must buy back Bitcoin, adding further buying pressure and potentially accelerating the rally.

That dynamic is why some traders question the move’s durability. Prominent trader @astronomer_zero described the move above $80,000 as a potential bull trap, dismissing it as a temporary “funny little pump” rather than the start of a sustained uptrend.

$btc The final trap has arrived ✅. I shorted here just above 80k.Alright. The trap has arrived.For the ones who have been living under a rock, this is the trap idea we have been planning and we are now seeing it in full effect.Talked about this trap yesterday as our 4… https://t.co/FkHjVKeIO0 pic.twitter.com/Ywa010ZLQR

The trader expects Bitcoin to revisit $75,000. He also outlined a potential pattern behind the move: “Pump → Range → Drop.” According to his assessment, Bitcoin is entering a consolidation phase that could precede another decline.

The rally shows how closely crypto still tracks macro and political sentiment alongside equities. Whether Bitcoin holds above $80,000 will indicate if the move reflects real demand or short-term positioning.

#5165. Ripple Executive Reveals Massive Growth of Company's London Hub

According to Ripple's UK and Europe Managing Director, Cassie Craddock, Ripple’s London office has expanded significantly over the last decade to become Ripple’s largest international hub outside the U.S.

“When we moved into our current office in 2018, there were 2 floors, and a lot of empty desks. Now we’re on 3 floors and full on all the desks. We’re now over capacity,” she wrote on X.

Craddock's comments came after she took part in a fireside chat at the British Residence in New York as part of Ripple’s Swell conference.

She revealed that Ripple chose to focus on London early on, given the UK's strong capital markets, mature legal system, experienced regulators and concentration of financial talent.

However, she added that regulatory uncertainty had previously limited the company’s capacity to build more relationships with leading U.K. financial institutions.

Ripple's story in the U.K. capital began on March 21, 2016, when the San Francisco-based company officially announced the launch of its first office in London, located in Canary Wharf.

Back then, the expansion was meant to accommodate the growing interest of European banks in distributed ledger technology and cross-border settlement solutions.

Ripple incorporated its U.K. entity in April 2016 under the name Ripple Europe Ltd. (later changed to Ripple Markets UK Ltd. in August 2022).

Ripple continued to expand its ties to U.K. financial institutions in the following years.

In April 2018, Santander introduced its new international payments app, One Pay FX, which used Ripple’s xCurrent technology.

In February 2022, Ripple announced a partnership with London-based payments company Modulr to make it easier to send cross-border payments to the UK and Europe.

Moreover, Ripple has played a role in developing the research landscape for blockchain in the U.K. In January 2026, Ripple shared that it had pledged more than £5M to UK universities under its University Blockchain Research Initiative.

One of the major milestones for Ripple in the U.K. occurred on Jan. 9, when Ripple received an Electronic Money Institution license and cryptoasset registration from the Financial Conduct Authority. The authorizations enabled Ripple to extend its regulated cross-border payment services to UK institutions.

In February, Ripple also secured a partnership with Aviva Investors to explore the tokenization of traditional investment funds.

This work resulted in the launch of a tokenized share class of Aviva Investors’ £22bn Sterling Liquidity Fund on the XRP Ledger on Oct. 26.

#5163. Judge Orders Bitcoin Wallet Returned to Family of Convicted Canadian Killer

Judge Orders Bitcoin Wallet Returned to Family of Convicted Canadian Killer

A Canadian judge has ordered that a laptop containing a Bitcoin wallet be returned to the family of William Sandeson, who is serving a life sentence for the 2015 murder of fellow university student Taylor Samson during a drugs deal, Global News reported.

Justice John Bodurtha of the Nova Scotia Supreme Court ruled on Tuesday that prosecutors had not proved the cryptocurrency was linked to the killing. Sandeson, who appeared virtually at the hearing in Halifax, had filed a motion in April seeking the return of personal items seized when police searched his apartment after his arrest in 2015.

"The Crown has not met its burden to demonstrate the cryptocurrency was used as part of the drug transaction, proceeds of crime, or otherwise tainted by criminality," Bodurtha said, adding that its potential use in the deal was "hypothetical."

The judge did rule that about $3,600 (C$5,000) in cash found in Sandeson's apartment after the murder was "offence-related property," meaning it will not be returned.

Sandeson has said the Bitcoin was worth between about $5,800 and $7,200 (C$8,000 to C$10,000), according to Global News. A lawyer representing Samson's family in a separate wrongful death lawsuit, in which the cryptocurrency had been at the center, has said it could now be worth more than $144,000 (C$200,000). Global News did not report how much Bitcoin the wallet holds.

Sandeson, then 23 and about to start medical school at Dalhousie University, arranged for Samson, 22, to come to his apartment on August 15, 2015, to sell him cannabis that Sandeson had arranged to buy for C$40,000, according to prosecutors at his 2023 trial. Sandeson shot Samson, kept the drugs and the money, and later admitted to dumping his body in a river that feeds the Bay of Fundy. Samson's remains have never been found.

Sandeson was convicted of first-degree murder in 2017, but an appeals court overturned the verdict in 2020. At a second trial in 2023, he admitted to shooting Samson but said he acted in self-defense. A jury found him guilty of second-degree murder, which carries an automatic life sentence. He is serving his sentence in Quebec and will be eligible for parole in 2030.

Solana (SOL) has lost its grip on the psychologically important $100 level, and the selling pressure keeps building. After a strong September rally that pushed SOL above $120, the token has slid into a long correction, with sellers increasingly in control of daily trading.

On November 4, SOL was trading around $92.69, extending its drop from the October highs near $124.

On the daily timeframe, the picture keeps getting worse, with Solana trading below all of its major moving averages. SOL has also fallen below the longer-term moving average near $98, which acted as support during the September recovery.

The breakdown puts Solana in a weak spot, especially now that its shorter-term moving averages have started turning down.

The $98–$100 zone is now the closest resistance. A proper recovery above it could improve sentiment and let SOL test the $103–$108 area. As long as the price stays below these levels, the chart stays bearish.

The closest support sits near $90, where buyers could try to stop the drop. A clear break below it could send Solana toward $85 and then the $80 region. Reclaiming $100, on the other hand, would be the first real step toward rebuilding bullish momentum.

Ethereum (ETH) is trying to find its feet after a correction that wiped out much of its September rally. It's showing early signs of a bounce from recent lows, but the second-largest cryptocurrency still faces heavy resistance, so the short-term outlook is far from clear.

Ethereum is also hovering near its longer-term moving average around $2,300, which makes the current price zone key for the next move.

The Relative Strength Index (RSI) has dropped a lot from its October highs as bullish momentum faded. However, it's starting to flatten near the lower part of its range, which suggests selling pressure could be easing. That may support a short-term bounce, but a flat RSI alone doesn't confirm a trend reversal.

The closest support is around $2,250, followed by the psychologically important $2,200 level. A break below this zone could open the door to a deeper correction toward $2,100.

NEAR Protocol (NEAR) couldn't hold on to its October rally, and sellers are back in charge. NEAR is back in correction mode, with the price dropping below several important technical indicators.

According to the November 4 daily chart, ZEC is trading near $1,288 after bouncing from a recent dip toward the $1,150 region.

Trading volume is also moderate, so the latest bounce hasn't drawn the same crowd as September's explosive rally.

For now, Zcash looks stronger than many altcoins on the chart, but it still needs a confirmed breakout above $1,300 before the bulls can celebrate.

#5159. Binance Hack Recovery Hits 98%, Proof of Reserves Expands to 44 Tokens

Home » Crypto Market News » Binance Hack Recovery Hits 98%, Proof of Reserves Expands to 44 Tokens

Binance is restoring withdrawals for 62 tokens while expanding zero-knowledge reserve checks after historic breach.

Binance reported major progress in its recovery efforts following the October 29 historic hot wallet breach.

The crypto exchange said that its hack recovery has reached about 98% of affected users’ asset value following the $6.3 billion hack. The exchange is also reopening withdrawals for 62 tokens and expanding its zero-knowledge Proof-of-Reserves (PoR) coverage.

Cryptocurrency exchange Binance published its eighth recovery update on November 5, confirming that it has successfully replenished approximately 98% of the user asset value affected by the October 29 hack.

The exchange also noted that it will gradually restore withdrawals for 69 network-asset pairs covering 62 tokens across six blockchains starting at 04:00 UTC on November 5.

Ethereum accounts for 36 assets, followed by BNB Chain (24), Base (4), TRON (3), Polygon (1), and Sui (1).

Binance said it prioritized assets based on user demand over the past week, with each withdrawal pair subject to security and readiness checks. It also warned that some tokens may not be immediately available, and high traffic could cause processing delays.

Binance’s reported replenishment progress advanced from 90% on October 31 to 95% on November 1, reaching 98% in the November 4 report.

The exchange said it would cover all losses using corporate funds, including its Secure Asset Fund for Users (SAFU), Futures Insurance Fund and corporate treasury reserves, with no losses passed on to retail customers.

The remaining 2% consists of less liquid tokens that Binance is sourcing through open markets and over-the-counter (OTC) deals. The exchange said acquiring them could take longer due to limited liquidity, making large purchases more likely to cause price slippage and volatility.

Binance also expanded its Proof of Reserves coverage from 10 to 44 tokens, introducing zero-knowledge verification, which allows users to confirm their balances are backed at least 1:1.

The exchange said generating the cryptographic proofs would take an estimated 50 to 60 hours. Once complete, holders of the covered assets will be able to verify that Binance holds reserves equal to at least 100% of its customer liabilities.

Initial reports and blockchain analytics firms estimated that attackers stole about $6.3 billion in digital assets across 10 blockchains during an attack that lasted roughly 20 minutes.

Tether, Circle and other major stablecoin issuers froze an estimated $2.65 billion to $3 billion in stolen assets, or about 42% of the total funds drained, according to compliance and analytics reports. The remaining assets, including 18,270 BTC and more than 420,000 ETH, remain largely untouched in identified hacker wallets.

Findings released by the FBI attributed the breach to TraderTraitor, a hacking group linked to North Korea.

Binance’s replenishment and expanded PoR address the key question after a major exchange breach: whether user funds are fully backed. The outcome could influence how other exchanges approach reserve transparency.

#5158. Binance's CZ Sounds Alarm Over New Crypto Phishing Scam

Binance co-founder Changpeng Zhao (CZ) has warned about a malicious crypto phishing scheme that uses a fake Trust Wallet website to lure unsuspecting users into connecting their wallets.

CZ shared a warning about a fraudulent website with a fake domain that mimics the popular Trust Wallet website.

Attackers modified the website domain by replacing the “u” with “a” and changing the extension from “.com” to “.cam,” which may trick unsuspecting users who don’t carefully inspect the domain.

The malicious website tricks users into connecting their crypto wallets in order to review their transfers.

However, the site contains JavaScript code that attempts to scan wallet balances using a backend API in order to prepare transfers to specific wallet addresses.

The attacker’s setup works with multiple well-known crypto wallets such as Trust Wallet, Phantom, and OKX Wallet.

CZ’s warning comes shortly after a surge in crypto phishing attempts against wallet users.

On Nov. 3, cybersecurity firm Bitdefender announced that scammers had started using the recent Binance security breach to spread fake emails impersonating Trust Wallet.

The phishing emails suggested that Trust Wallet users were also impacted by the Binance security breach. Therefore, they instructed users to perform a mandatory wallet verification procedure.

The email messages warned that users would be suspended after Nov. 7 if they did not complete the wallet verification process.

Bitdefender said that the phishing scheme affected users in multiple countries, including the United States, United Kingdom, Australia, Ireland and South Africa.

The phishing messages led users to fake verification websites where their wallet credentials or recovery data could be leaked.

Earlier this year, cybersecurity analysts from CYFIRMA spotted another phishing attack against Trust Wallet users. The attackers used malicious QR codes on Telegram to target Trust Wallet users.

#5157. Midterms Blue Wave Makes $917K for One Polymarket Trader, If Alaska Doesn’t Flip

Midterms Blue Wave Makes $917K for One Polymarket Trader, If Alaska Doesn’t Flip

A "blue wave” swept the U.S. midterms as the Democrats took control of both the House of Representatives and the Senate, in a crushing blow to President Donald Trump.

Polymarket predictor 0xA2 is the platform's most profitable trader over the past 24 hours, according to the site's daily leaderboard. They are currently $917,000 in profit, although most of that is still in an active position that has a real (ableit small) chance of upset.

The user wagered $609,644 on the Republicans not winning any Senate swing states when odds sat at just 36%, a market which has since surged to 87%. This turned his stake in to $1.48 million, putting him $860,000 in the green.

Its important to remember: these are unrealized gains and could drop at any moment as 0xA2 waits for Alaska's final verdict. Polymarket traders put a 14% chance on an upset—and while this may seem small, the site has seen similarly sized upsets recently—as in the case of Brazil's presidential election two weeks ago.

0xA2 also profited $106,115 by predicting that the Democrats would win the Texas Senate—profits that have been realized. Oddly, the user's second largest active position is $1.42 million on the U.S. not confirming that aliens exist before 2027.

"Election night showed how quickly round-the-clock markets move when traditional exchanges are closed. As polls began closing at 6pm ET, Kalshi’s odds of a Democratic sweep went from about 74% to 99%," Kelly Ye, Head of Research at digital asset management firm Talos, said in a note shared with Decrypt. "Prediction-market trading peaked between 8pm and 10pm ET, led by a small number of large trades."

It was a big day for Polymarket users profiting, with the top 10 users on the platform profiting more than $170,000, per the leaderboard. To put that into perspective, on a fairly random day in March this year, the top daily winner on Polymarket profited $154,000, according to the WayBackMachine, and the 10th biggest winner that day gained $29,700.

The midterms attracted major activity on leading prediction markets Polymarket and Kalshi. According to Precition.com data, midterm markets attracted more than $546 million in notional volume over the past two weeks.

"The 2024 election was the breakthrough moment for prediction markets. It was their first major real-world test and the event that really drove volume and open interest," Dash, the pseudonymous data lead at Prediction.com, told Decrypt. "Today, sports dominate trading volume, but Politics is still the key driving factor for open interest."

During the 2024 presidential race, Polymarket became what Bernstein analysts called the world’s largest election prediction market, with its flagship market topping $3.6 billion in volume. Maybe more importantly, predictors favored Trump for most of the campaign, while traditional polls kept him neck and neck with Kamala Harris. Prediction market users took this as a sign that prediction markets are more accurate than traditional political polling.

"Increasingly, people are looking for accurate forecasts on election results," Dash said, "and prediction markets are one place they can be found."

Those forecasts aren't always accurate, though. Just two weeks ago, Polymarket predictors whiffed Brazil's presidential election, with users giving Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva about a 20% chance of winning ahead of election day, before he ultimately beat Flávio Bolsonaro to take a fourth term.

Experts told Decrypt at the time that Brazil’s prediction market ban had left the odds in the hands of traders with little local knowledge to correct the odds. Plus, experts warned that a 20% chance of something happening is still a very real chance.

"Ultimately, something that has an 80% chance of happening does not happen 20% of the time," Luke Nolan, senior researcher at CoinShares, said.

So that 14% chance that 0xA2 loses his $860,000 in profit should be taken seriously.

#5156. Zcash targets 67% shorter block times – Can ZEC overcome funding uncertainty?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Zcash [ZEC] is preparing a major network upgrade that will reduce block time. The network upgrade (NU7) is designed to shorten the time it takes for blocks to be processed.

Set to be implemented on the 6th of November, this new system will enable Zcash to decrease block processing times by approximately 67%, from 75 seconds to 25 seconds.

Simply, the direct result of this change will likely be the ability for users to finalize transactions significantly faster than before. In addition, it will enable the network to process more total activity, all without altering the amount of ZEC that is added to circulation each day.

Notably, there are two other aspects of the upgrade in need of attention. First, the Sprout pool will no longer exist. Second, 60% of every transaction fee collected by the network will now go into a deferred reserve.

However, how successful these upgrades are in creating the desired effect will depend on whether the faster confirmation rates create increased adoption on-chain.

Although NU7 improves the way Zcash’s network operates, the Zcash funding model faces a separate challenge.

Who gets to control the future funding for development was a subject of debate for weeks prior to the 3rd of November. This was when Zcash Community Grants announced that it had withdrawn its proposal to direct about 98,000 ZEC into future miner rewards.

Notably, that amount represented about half of its entire treasury.

The proposal would have drawn 36,200 ZEC from ZCG’s 91,500-ZEC treasury, with the remainder coming from reduced future allocations through 2028. However, its withdrawal leaves the original funding structure intact.

As such, ZCG will continue receiving 8% of newly issued ZEC, equivalent to roughly 144 ZEC daily. Miners retain 80%, while 12% enters the coinholder-controlled lockbox.

Although withdrawing the proposal leaves current distributions intact, alternative proposals continue to leave long-term funding for development uncertain.

Beyond governance uncertainty, Zcash’s institutional demand also shows signs of weakening, although recent flows suggest a possible shift. ZEC spot ETFs recorded three consecutive days of withdrawals, with weekly net outflows reaching $7.21 million.

However, the 4th of November saw a $3.43 million inflow into ZEC spot ETFs, ending the streak of net outflows. The inflow increased total cumulative net inflows to $217.73 million and net assets to $756.83 million. Volume also hit $59.45 million.

While there was an increase in net inflows recently, recent outflows indicate that institutions become more cautious. Additionally, while ETFs still hold 3.43% of outstanding circulation of ZEC, institutions do maintain a significant presence in spite of decreased demand.

#5155. Gate Pioneers Proof of Security (PoSEC), Achieving 104.58% Cold Storage Coverage to Solidify Asset Security

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Gate has officially introduced Proof of Security (PoSEC), a new asset security verification framework, becoming the first digital asset trading platform in the industry to launch a PoSEC security verification system. Since its founding in 2013, Gate has consistently placed user asset security at the forefront of its priorities. With the introduction of PoSEC, Gate extends security verification beyond reserve adequacy to include asset storage structures and risk coverage capabilities, further solidifying safeguards for user assets and providing a new practical approach to advancing industry security standards.

Unlike Proof of Reserves (PoR), which primarily verifies the adequacy of asset reserves, PoSEC places additional emphasis on asset storage security. By publicly disclosing the distribution of assets between platform cold storage and hot wallets, PoSEC provides visibility into asset storage structures and their ability to cover potential losses under extreme scenarios.

PoSEC introduces the cold storage coverage ratio as a key metric to measure the extent to which cold storage covers total user assets. A coverage ratio ≥ 100% means that, under the applicable calculation methodology, cold storage alone would be sufficient to cover all user assets even if all assets held in hot wallets were lost. PoR and PoSEC serve complementary purposes, extending asset security verification from reserve adequacy to storage security and risk coverage, while providing users with more comprehensive and transparent information about asset security.

According to the snapshot taken at 00:00 (UTC) on November 4, 2026, Gate’s cold storage coverage ratio reached 104.58%, with cold storage and hot wallet assets accounting for 88.26% and 11.74% of total assets, respectively. Cold storage assets include digital assets held offline and traditional assets under independent custody. To qualify, these assets must meet requirements such as not being directly used for routine deposits and withdrawals, being held offline or under independent custody, requiring multi-party authorization for transfers, and being independently verifiable.

The current data is undergoing verification by an independent third-party audit firm. The review covers asset authenticity and control, valuation and calculation methodologies, and coverage ratio calculations. The audit report will be published on Gate’s official website upon completion, enhancing the transparency and credibility of asset security disclosures.

Gate has long been committed to digital asset security and transparency. In 2020, Gate was among the first in the industry to introduce an open-source Proof of Reserves (PoR) mechanism and a Merkle Tree-based asset verification solution, establishing itself as an early contributor to reserve transparency in the industry. Gate subsequently incorporated technologies such as zk-SNARKs to enhance its asset reserve verification framework.

As of October 18, 2026, Gate’s total reserves amounted to approximately $8.688 billion, with an overall reserve ratio of 118.49%. The platform also employs a range of security mechanisms, including hot and cold wallet segregation, multi-signature technology, Multi-Party Computation (MPC), dynamic risk monitoring, and zero-trust access control, to establish a multi-layered security framework covering asset reserves, storage management, and risk protection.

From PoR, which verifies reserve adequacy, to PoSEC, which verifies asset storage security and risk coverage capabilities, Gate is progressively building a multi-layered security framework encompassing asset reserves, storage management, risk protection, and public verification. Looking ahead, Gate will continue to uphold its security-first principle, further develop PoSEC standards and verification mechanisms, and collaborate with more industry participants to enhance asset security and transparency, laying a stronger security foundation for the long-term, sustainable development of the digital asset industry.

Learn more: https://www.gate.com/proof-of-security

Gate, founded in 2013 by Dr. Han, is one of the world’s leading cryptocurrency and integrated financial services platforms, spanning financial and everyday use cases including asset management, trading and payments. Serving over 62 million users globally, it supports trading across 5,400+ digital assets and 12,800+ stock assets and ETFs, while providing access to a comprehensive range of TradFi assets, including metals, stocks, indices, forex, and commodities to meet users’ end-to-end needs across asset holding, trading, and spending. As an industry benchmark, Gate was among the first platforms to implement 100% Proof of Reserves. Its ecosystem includes Gate Money, Gate Wallet, Gate Ventures, Gate for AI Agent, and a wide range of products and services.
