#5154. Why Shiba Inu (SHIB) Didn't Join Rally: Bitcoin (BTC) $80,000 Surge Leaves Altcoins Behind

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Bitcoin (BTC) is back on its feet above the psychologically important $80,000 level after dipping to about $75,600 with the help of a fresh buying interest. Shiba Inu (SHIB) isn't joining the party, though, and stays under substantial selling pressure.

BTC was trading around $80,300 on November 5, up more than 3% over 24 hours. Ether did even better, gaining about 5% to roughly $2,421, so the weakness looks specific to SHIB rather than to the whole altcoin market.

The jump looks like a short squeeze: about $378 million in short positions were liquidated within a single hour, and total liquidations reached around $747 million, according to CoinGlass. Spot Bitcoin ETFs also stopped bleeding. Outflows of about $1.16 billion across October 30, November 2 and November 3 gave way to a $74.6 million net inflow on November 4, according to Farside, although BlackRock's IBIT still lost $201.2 million that day.

Unfortunately, short covering alone doesn't prove fresh demand, and CryptoQuant analyst CryptoZeno argues the correction may not be over, pointing to the Bitcoin Cycle Extremes Index sitting at 15.8% on its 30-day average.

On the daily chart, buyers absorbed the selling near the 200-day moving average in the $76,000–$77,000 support region, and BTC then climbed back above its shorter-term moving averages. The relative strength index (RSI) is around 51, up from weaker readings, so momentum is improving, but a bullish reversal isn't confirmed yet.

XRP, Solana and BNB added roughly 2–4% over the same 24 hours, but Shiba Inu hasn't managed a proper recovery. SHIB is trading near $0.00000475, well below its September highs around $0.00000620.

The token has also broken below its ascending support trendline, invalidating the previously positive technical structure, and it stays below its major moving averages, which increasingly act as resistance rather than support. The closest resistance zone is around $0.00000510–$0.00000530, while stronger resistance remains near $0.00000550.

Although the RSI is approaching oversold territory, there's not enough evidence of proper accumulation yet.

#5153. USDC Issuer Circle Quietly Cuts 5% of Staff in Profitable Quarter

USDC Issuer Circle Quietly Cuts 5% of Staff in Profitable Quarter

Circle, the company behind the USDC stablecoin, has cut about 5% of its workforce, according to its third-quarter earnings release on Wednesday.

The reduction was "designed to better align our organizational structure with our operating model and strategic priorities," the company said in footnotes to its financial tables. Circle did not say how many employees were affected or which teams were cut.

It recorded $9.1 million in restructuring expenses in the quarter, mostly cash payments for notice periods and severance, employee benefits and related costs. Those were partly offset by a $5.7 million reversal of stock-based compensation, as unvested shares held by departing employees were forfeited.

The cuts came in a profitable quarter. Circle reported net income from continuing operations of $226 million, up 5% from a year earlier, on total revenue and reserve income of $865 million, up 17%. Adjusted EBITDA jumped 85% year-on-year to $317 million.

Much of that growth came from outside Circle's core business. Other revenue rose to $207 million, including $182 million from the presale of ARC, the token for its Arc blockchain. Reserve income, the interest Circle earns on the assets backing USDC and its largest source of revenue, fell 8% to $658 million as interest rates on those reserves declined.

The average amount of USDC in circulation rose 8% from a year earlier to $73.3 billion, but ended the quarter flat at $73.9 billion. USDC's share of the U.S. dollar stablecoin market slipped to 27%, according to the company.

CEO Jeremy Allaire did not mention the job cuts in his comments on the results, or in a tweet on Wednesday calling Q3 "a big quarter for Circle." He said USDC carried 79% of all stablecoin transaction volume, up from 66% a year earlier, and that Circle's payments network ended the quarter at $41 billion in annualized volume, up 15-fold. "In January, the GENIUS Act takes effect and regulated digital dollars go mainstream," Allaire wrote. "Every layer of our platform is live and ready for it."

→ USDC carried 79% of all stablecoin transaction volume, up from 66% a year ago, and $13.9T moved onchain (+49%)

→ A record $208B of USDC was minted and redeemed, about $2.3B every day

→ Arc mainnet went live, with 100+ institutions and apps… pic.twitter.com/euVfyHBUXX

— Jeremy Allaire (@jerallaire) November 4, 2026

The cuts come as Circle expands. In September, it launched the public mainnet of its Arc blockchain, with BlackRock, DTCC and Visa among its permissioned validators. Allaire said in October that Circle plans to enter about 40 emerging and developing markets, and the company has since teamed up with payments-technology firm ACI Worldwide to pilot USDC settlement on Colombia's instant-payments network.

Circle is the latest in a string of crypto companies to cut staff this year, citing weaker markets, artificial intelligence or both.

Coinbase cut 14% of its workforce in May, with CEO Brian Armstrong pointing to the crypto downturn and the company's adoption of AI. The Ethereum Foundation cut 20% of its staff in June as part of a reorganization, while custody firm BitGo cut 15% that month to focus on AI and stablecoins.

Exchange Luno cut a fifth of its global staff in July, citing automation, and crypto ETF issuer Bitwise cut 14% of its staff in August.

Trump-linked tokens fell sharply as XRP & Bitcoin rallied, highlighting a divide between politically tied digital assets & the broader crypto market.

Trump-linked tokens suffered sharp losses while XRP and Bitcoin rallied, according to Money Maker a.k.a Avi Lev, a crypto-market commentator who argues that scrutiny of the president’s business interests need not derail broader crypto legislation.

Avi Lev’s central claim: bipartisan support for digital assets could matter more than the fortunes of individual political ventures.

The video frames these moves around the 2026 U.S. midterm elections, citing Stand With Crypto’s tally of 245 elected pro-crypto candidates.

However, the transcript does not establish its publication date or independently substantiate those election results, and it gives conflicting percentages for pro-crypto winners.

Avi Lev reports declines of roughly 19% for the TRUMP token, 14% for MELANIA and 4% for WLFI. Trump Media shares reportedly fell about 24% in the session discussed, with the commentator noting that earnings also contributed to the pressure.

The YouTube video clip connects those losses to anticipated Democratic investigations into Trump’s crypto ventures and other business dealings. That explanation is the host’s interpretation, rather than a demonstrated cause of the price moves.

Avi Lev quotes Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse describing a “new Congress with the same mission,” pointing to support for crypto on both sides of the aisle. The video also cites reporting that House Democrats want to revive their preferred CLARITY Act provisions within broader market-structure legislation.

Neither claim establishes that a bill will actually be acticated. The commentator instead raises a narrower question: whether a more restrictive compromise would offer the industry more certainty than continued legislative deadlock.

The immediate chart level sits at approximately $1.34, which Lev treats as resistance, not a confirmed breakout or price forecast.

For the wider market, the distinction is consequential: political pressure on Trump-linked assets and progress toward crypto rules are separate risks, even when they move prices on the same day.

#5150. Polygon Plans Hard Fork to Seize $6.5 Million From Polymarket and Binance Hackers

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Polygon developers now propose a step that has long been considered an absolute taboo in the crypto industry. They put to a vote a plan to rescue Binance and Polymarket money, and this plan is, to say it mildly, radical — zero out the hackers’ wallets at the protocol level. Ongoing cybersecurity crisis shows no signs of stopping, and now the industry is being faced with an uncomfortable choice: save systemic players or protect the inviolability of Web3’s main rule that transactions cannot be reversed. The Polygon PoS hard fork involves seizing 70.49 million POL tokens ($6.5 million at the time of the attack) from the hacker addresses to return them to the victims.

The crisis unfolded at the end of October after two independent hacks. The stolen assets were scattered across five wallets (23.86 million POL was taken from Polymarket, 46.63 million from Binance). Yes, Polygon validators immediately blacklisted the addresses, cutting off the outflow, but this is just an attempt to hold back the dam with bare hands. Manual blocking is a temporary plug, and the authors of the proposal openly admit it.

The hacker is not giving up either and is continuously bombarding the network with transactions carrying wild fees of up to 219,000 POL, counting on automation or a validator with a weakness for easy money to let the operation through for a windfall profit. Things have gone as far as direct attempts to bribe validators with cash, and the hacker simply ignored on-chain negotiations. The developers’ logic is simple: keeping millions in a manual “ban” forever is a dead end that leaves the entire network under constant fire.

No one has access to the hacker’s private keys, so Polygon is changing the consensus rules themselves. Should the hard fork get approval at a certain block, the hackers’ balances will be zeroed out, and an equivalent amount will be mirror-credited to verified Binance and Polymarket wallets — without any token printing.

Polygon Labs is well aware that such an administrative step completely negates the very idea of decentralization.

This plan inevitably triggers a strong sense of the notorious The DAO hard fork of 2016. Back then, to save the young Ethereum ecosystem, developers also had to go through a painful split of the community and ignore the “Code is Law” principle. But Polygon Labs hopes that in their case consensus among validators is already rock-solid, so they are narrowing the framework of the precedent as much as possible, positioning the fork as an exclusively one-time action.

The fate of the millions in POL token is being decided right now in a community vote. The largest validators are already in favor, since both platforms are critically important to Polygon’s survival. If the plan is approved, the software will be released after two weeks of testing, and exchanges and node operators will have to urgently update. Those who refuse to switch to the new code face automatic isolation on the old branch of the blockchain — with no connection to the main network.

#5149. Bitcoin Shrugs Off Midterms as Experts Say Democratic Sweep Was 'Priced In'

Bitcoin Shrugs Off Midterms as Experts Say Democratic Sweep Was 'Priced In'

Democrats flipped both the House of Representatives and the Senate in the U.S. midterms, handing President Donald Trump a major political setback.

Experts say this was not the "preferred outcome" for crypto markets and that it has increased "uncertainty" for future pro-crypto policy. However, after a brief dip, Bitcoin is up 1.6% over the past 24 hours and now sits at $78,277, per Decrypt data. Broadly, almost every top 100 crypto token by market cap is in the green for the day.

"I think the Democratic sweep was largely priced in going into the midterms," André Dragosch, head of research at Bitwise Europe, told Decrypt. "The downside risks were fairly limited going into these midterms as sentiment was already very bearish and there were already signs of seller exhaustion again. So, it is no surprise that we have seen this kind of V-shaped recovery on an intraday basis during the midterms."

Polymarket put odds of the Democratic Party sweeping both the House and Senate during the midterms at 65.5% at the start of the week. Dragosch said these odds meant that markets weren't shocked by Tuesday's results.

James Butterfill, head of research at CoinShares, agreed. "My sense is that Bitcoin had largely priced in a Democratic sweep, leaving room for a relief rally as uncertainty clears," he said in comments shared with Decrypt. "Therefore, the anticipated result need not trigger another sell-off."

"Crypto policy did not decide the midterms. The economy, gas prices, the Iran situation, and Trump's unmet promises did," Heslin Kim, co-founder and CBO of Zenith, told Decrypt. "Crypto policy was irrelevant to the broader voting pool, but it now has the baggage of all the egregious activity that was stirred up in our ecosystem associated with the Trump family."

Tim Sun, senior analyst at HashKey, told Decrypt that the results do "increase the uncertainty surrounding the progress of crypto policy," but that Bitcoin's price action means that the market doesn't interpret it as a "major negative catalyst."

Specifically, this may be because the midterm results don't mean the Democrats can roll back existing pro-crypto guidelines set out by the SEC and CFTC, Dragosch said. However, it does mean that going forward, the market cannot rely as heavily on Trump's "political tailwinds for momentum," Sun added.

It also complicates the picture for the Clarity Act, which would establish rules for crypto markets. The bill has been in limbo since it was introduced in 2025, with many Democrats opposing it due to Trump's crypto conflicts of interest.

Dmitry Machikhin, CEO of BitOK, predicted in a note shared with Decrypt that the Clarity Act will remain in limbo well into 2027, thanks to the midterm results.

"The greater concern is slower crypto reform," Butterfill said. "CLARITY faces a harder path, while investigations into Trump-linked ventures could consume political capital and generate uncomfortable headlines across the sector. But the administration remains in place, and existing regulatory progress, including GENIUS, does not simply disappear."

He expects U.S. exchanges, token issuers and businesses that rely on new regulatory permissions to be more sensitive to the result than Bitcoin, and said established, compliant operators could even gain if scrutiny falls disproportionately on weaker competitors.

"A Democratic Congress may even be good for the industry in one way," Marcin Kaźmierczak, co-founder and COO of RedStone, told Decrypt. "Crypto shouldn't depend on one political camp. Infrastructure has to work for whoever is in power and bring most value to the ecosystem participants. Finance is changing, and politics will not stop it."

Despite the switch-up in the House and Senate, Dragosch expects little to change over the next two years.

"I personally expect more disputes, disagreements, and simply more of 'nothing stops this train,'" he said, pointing to a lack of significant progress to rein in fiscal deficits. "We will probably remain in a fiscally dominant environment with increasing fiscal risks, which I think is ultimately a tailwind for hard assets like Bitcoin."

Instead, he believes macro factors "remain in the driver's seat for now." Specifically, he points to a potential upcoming pivot from the Federal Reserve as a response to "increasing risk" around the AI trade.

"This will likely cause renewed interest in debasement-resistant assets like Bitcoin," Dragosch said, "and will continue to drive institutional adoption via ETPs and treasury companies as well."

Butterfill also pointed to macro factors. "For Bitcoin, real yields, dollar liquidity and ETP flows still carry more weight than the elections," he said. "I would temper expectations for the pace of U.S. reform, while remaining constructive on institutional adoption. How Trump responds to being constrained is the less predictable part."

Long-time Shiba Inu community member Mazrael shares a security reminder with the Shiba Inu community, particularly iPhone users, in an X post.

Mazrael said Google's Threat Intelligence team detailed "DarkSword," an iOS exploit chain reportedly capable of taking full control of iPhones running iOS 18.4 through iOS 18.7 just by visiting a booby-trapped website.

One of the spyware tools associated with "DarkSword" reportedly targets cryptocurrency wallet data, device keychains and also messages, photos, and passwords. So far, "DarkSword" has been used in various targeted attacks by spyware vendors and suspected state hackers, but Google warns other attackers may be using it too.

SHIBARMY SECURITY REMINDER: iPhone users Google's Threat Intelligence team has detailed "DarkSword", an iOS exploit chain that can fully take over iPhones running iOS 18.4 to 18.7, just by visiting a booby-trapped website. One of the spy tools used with it steals cryptocurrency wallet data and device keychains, plus messages, photos and passwords. But Google warns other attackers may be using it too. ✅ WHAT TO DO 1️⃣ Update iOS now: Settings > General > Software Update (all the bugs are fixed in iOS 26.3 or later) 2️⃣ Can't update? This raises concerns for cryptocurrency holders, particularly those who access wallet-related services and other sensitive accounts through their mobile devices.

Mazrael highlighted steps to be taken, urging iPhone users to install the latest available iOS updates, noting that the identified vulnerabilities have been addressed in iOS 26.3 and later. Users unable to update are urged to activate Apple's Lockdown Mode and avoid suspicious links, particularly those involving cryptocurrency.

For individuals who suspect their devices or wallet credentials may have been compromised, the reminder recommends creating a new wallet on a clean, updated device and transferring funds. Exposed seed phrases should never be reused.

Binance has resumed withdrawals in phases, with that of SHIB on the Ethereum network also enabled.

In a recent post, binance warns that opportunistic scammers may try to take advantage of this period as withdrawals resume in phases.

Scammers may impersonate Binance through DMs, emails, group chats and even small transfers with a message attached, offering to "speed up withdrawals," give "priority processing," help to "claim compensation," or move assets into a "Binance vault," with users urged to take precautions.

Users should remember that Binance will never contact them first about their account or funds, nor offer or sell them a place in the withdrawal queue or charge a fee to unlock a network or asset.

Ripple Prime’s weekend trading service offers institutions exposure to more than 100 markets, with RLUSD accepted as collateral.

Ripple Prime is offering institutions weekend exposure to traditional markets, but XRP is not named in the launch described by popular wealth commentator Kamilah Stevenson.

In her video, Dr. Stevenson examines a service covering more than 100 markets, arguing that round-the-clock trading could eventually broaden demand for Ripple’s digital-asset infrastructure.

The immediate connection is to RLUSD, Ripple’s dollar stablecoin, which clients can post as collateral. The case for XRP is less direct, resting on potential lending services, activity on the XRP Ledger and demand for cross-currency transfers.

According to Stevenson, Ripple Prime launched the service on October 15, offering swaps tied to stocks, indices, ETFs, metals, energy and currencies.

These contracts provide price exposure without ownership of the underlying assets; they do not mean conventional stock exchanges have opened for weekend trading.

The YouTube epsidode says pricing comes from contracts listed on Hyperliquid, with Ripple Prime serving as the institutional counterparty and handling the blockchain connection. Clients can cross-margin positions, potentially reducing collateral requirements by offsetting exposures.

Kamilah Stevenson identifies market maker Virtu as an early user and says the offering operates under Ripple Prime’s British Virgin Islands license. Broader regional availability remains a development to watch, rather than something investors should assume is already available.

“A market that runs 24-7 needs money that moves 24-7,” she says. Stablecoin collateral can move outside conventional bank-wire hours, although that does not eliminate liquidity constraints or counter-party risk.

Dr. Stevenson then points to potential XRP-backed short-term loans and the XRP Ledger’s account reserves and transaction fees as possible sources of utility. She also argues that weekend trading could strengthen the case for XRP as a bridge between crypto & fiat currencies.

That argument needs a distinction: exposure to a foreign stock index does not, by itself, establish that a swap requires settlement in that market’s currency or that XRP must handle any conversion. The video provides no confirmation that this trading service uses XRP for settlement.

Her broader thesis is that more weekend markets could spread selling pressure that currently falls heavily on crypto. That remains a possibility, not an established outcome; additional venues can also introduce thin liquidity & new leverage risks.

Four funds attracted money on November 4, with FBTC leading inflows while IBIT remained the sole fund recording withdrawals.

US spot Bitcoin ETF inflows reached $75 million on Nov. 4, 2026. The first positive session after three consecutive days of withdrawals depended on Fidelity's FBTC overcoming another negative reading from BlackRock's IBIT.

FBTC recorded net inflows of $237 million, according to Farside Investors' daily table. IBIT recorded net outflows of $201 million.

Three other funds also attracted money: ARKB recorded net inflows of $20 million, BITB recorded net inflows of $15 million and BTCO recorded net inflows of $4 million.

#5145. XRP Casino Transfers: Destination Tags, Transaction Costs, and Common Mistakes

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

At many crypto casino cashiers, XRP has earned itself a distinguished place. It’s a popular way to manage transfers, which settle in seconds and cost virtually nothing, at least from the platform’s perspective. Sounds good, right?

But there are features of XRP that tend to confuse and dismay unsuspecting users. Because most casinos’ deposits require a destination tag, there are nuanced elements that require additional consideration. We’ll take a look at how to use XRP casino transfers and under what conditions they might be right for you.

Running on the XRP Ledger, a network designed for fast and low-cost payments, XRP transactions are ideal for the casino setting because they are fast and relatively forgiving from a fee perspective. Since crypto casinos exist primarily to be affordable, fast ways to move money, these are definitely pluses.

Your deposits appear fast, and you can start playing at the casino without the lengthy wait that might otherwise follow from certain cryptocurrencies or traditional currencies.

And because other cryptocurrencies charge fees that could easily come to $10 to $15, the much more modest fee structure of XRP is often an inviting alternative. If you’re comparing XRP with stablecoin payment options, USDT Casinos is another resource to explore, keeping deposit networks, withdrawal fees, and cashier requirements in mind.

As mentioned in the introduction, most casinos will require a shared XRP address to receive deposits. The destination tag is what tells the casino which account should receive payment.

You could think of the address as an apartment building, while the tag is the apartment number. The payment itself might reach the building, but without that apartment number, what good is it really doing anyone?

Destination tags on this platform are always presented as numbers and can be up to 10 digits long. They will sometimes be expressed as memos or memo IDs by the casino.

Note also that XRP addresses always begin with the letter R. These types of details sound small, but can be genuinely impactful for players trying to move money.

Beginning at the casino cashier, you’ll peruse the drop-down menu looking for XRP as your preferred payment method. Naturally, not every casino will be able to facilitate these types of transactions, but we have included a list of ones that do, presented in a table within this article.

Once you’ve chosen this as your method, the casino will display a deposit address, along with a destination tag. Your job is to copy the values as they’re presented on the screen exactly.

Before you copy those, it’s good to compare the tag one more time against the cashier page, because a single wrong digit can and will completely spoil the transaction. Lost deposits in the world of crypto payments are almost never recoverable, so it’s important to get things right.

Withdrawals are essentially just a reversal of the process described above. Personal wallets like Xaman will typically not require a tag, as the address itself can identify your account. However, an exchange account will almost always require a destination tag.

Copy the tag from the exchange’s XRP deposit page, and then enter it within the casino withdrawal. Casino processing will play a role too. It’s important to understand what they have to do on their end to get things rolling.

This can include identity checks and manual reviews that may make the transaction take longer. These are particularly common for newer accounts.

XRP transaction costs are often just a fraction of a penny, though the actual number may rise and fall a bit depending on circumstances, usually related to network traffic. In either case, it’s still going to be a much cheaper option than the majority of alternative coins.

Note that every XRP Ledger account must hold a minimum reserve to function. The base reserve at the time of writing is one XRP. Short of this figure, you’ll be unable to use an account. So it’s important to maintain this reserve.

Is XRP worth your time and attention? An increasingly large number of people are answering that question with an affirmative. That doesn’t mean it’s right for you, but it does mean it has a good set of features that are well tailored to the needs of many online casino players.

It’s important to remember that when it comes to online casino crypto transactions, investment value is not the consideration. You’re looking for a coin that is fast and forgiving in terms of fee structure. You get both things with XRP.

#5144. South Korea Announces Delay in Crypto Taxation Policy

South Korea announced a delay in the introduction of its cryptocurrency capital gains tax until 2029, extending the tax-free period by two years as authorities plan on strengthening regulatory oversight and connecting data from exchanges with the United States, yna.co.kr reports.

Finance Minister Lee Hyoung-il announced the proposal Thursday, acknowledging that the government would be postponing the controversial tax for a fourth time already.

Originally, the policy was scheduled to become active in January 2022: it would impose a 22% tax, local income tax on all cryptocurrency-related gains that exceed 2.5 million won.

While the proposed delay could provide a relief for the South Korean cryptocurrency market and even support the domestic trading activity, it remains a substantial risk factor for both local and overseas investors.

Officials argue that implementing the tax before international information-sharing systems become operational could disadvantage local exchanges and complicate enforcement against overseas trading activity.

Future tax liabilities aren't going to be eliminated, additional time to develop a comprehensive taxation network is more likely to enforce even more limitations on investors in the future, rather than a premature implementation with various legal loopholes.

The government also intends to introduce provisions allowing investors to carry forward cryptocurrency investment losses for up to three years, potentially reducing taxable gains in subsequent periods.

South Korea is also going to establish a separate working group that is going to further refine taxation rules and hopefully simplify reporting requirements for investors, considering the complexity of reporting digital assets transactions and traders on exchanges.

Legislative amendments are expected to reach the National Assembly in 2027.

Authorities reported the continuous coordinating with the Financial Services Commission to accelerate digital assets legislation, providing more transparency and investors protection.

For the industry in general, the proposed extension is more of a transition stage that should improve the competitiveness of the digital assets market, despite introducing stricter tax enforcement. Unfortunately for retail investors, it creates another barrier that might push fresh funds further away from the industry.

#5143. Morning Minute: CFTC Clears Stock Perps, Gets Sued the Same Day

Morning Minute: CFTC Clears Stock Perps, Gets Sued the Same Day

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

⚖️ CFTC Clears Stock Perps, Gets Sued the Same Day

A perpetual future is a leveraged bet on a price that never expires. You hold it as long as you can meet margin and keep paying a funding rate. It’s analog product would be options in TradFi, with a few differences (primarily no expiration date). Crypto traders have used them for years, mostly on offshore venues like Binance and onchain platforms like Hyperliquid, because U.S. law had no clear home for the product.

On Tuesday the CFTC said Americans can now trade them on individual stocks.

Hours later, Wall Street sued to stop it.

The approval came as a statement from CFTC Chairman Michael Selig. Equity perpetual futures are now tradable on CFTC-registered exchanges, classified as security futures products under the Commodity Futures Modernization Act of 2000, which puts them under joint CFTC and SEC oversight.

The list of venues that can actually do this is short, and it was assembled quietly. A firm needs to be a CFTC-designated contract market and separately notice-registered with the SEC as a national securities exchange for security futures products. Seven entities hold both today: Cboe, CME, Coinbase, Kalshi, Bitnomial, Nadex, and ICE Futures U.S. Five of those seven filed inside a single 30-day window in September.

Kalshi is furthest along, with a filing covering 56 to 58 names and a launch expected within days. Coinbase has filed to list perps on Apple, Tesla and Nvidia. So those two are immediate winners. But Hyperliquid and Lighter, the top crypto perps protocols, are next in line for potential CFTC approvals and are likely future winners as well (LIT +10% on the day).

Then came the lawsuit. A new group called the Capital Markets Alliance filed in the Southern District of New York, naming Cboe, Citadel Securities and the Healthy Markets Association among its members. Its argument runs on two tracks.

The group wants the policy eliminated. The American Securities Association piled on separately, pointing out that Congress banned selling security-based swaps to retail investors at all.

A CFTC spokesman called the suit “troubling,” adding that “our markets should be governed by law, not litigation campaigns intended to block innovation.” The CME already tried this once, suing over the CFTC’s crypto perps approval on the theory that perpetuals are swaps rather than futures. That one went nowhere.

Well, remember that old phrase: “First they ignore you, then they laugh at you, then they fight you, then you win."

We’re at the fight stage. Next stop, winning.

#5142. Best Decentralized Video Streaming Platforms: 5 YouTube Alternatives

Home » Cryptopedia » Best Decentralized Video Streaming Platforms: 5 YouTube Alternatives

Explore the top decentralized video platforms, compare their features, and discover alternatives that give creators more control and freedom.

YouTube dominates online video, but it isn’t the only option for watching, sharing, and publishing content. Decentralized video platforms offer an alternative model, giving creators and communities more control over content distribution, hosting, and, in some cases, monetization.

Unlike traditional video platforms that rely on centralized infrastructure, decentralized services can use peer-to-peer networks, blockchain technology, or federated servers to distribute and manage video content.

But which platforms are actually worth using?

In this guide, we explore seven decentralized video platforms and YouTube alternatives, including DTube, Odysee, PeerTube, 3Speak, DLive, Theta, and Flixxo. We’ll compare their underlying technology, publishing features, monetization options, and limitations to help you choose the right platform.

Decentralized video platforms distribute video hosting, storage, and delivery across independent servers or networks rather than relying on a single company like YouTube. This gives creators and communities more control over content distribution, ownership, and monetization.

They use different technologies. Blockchain records transactions and can reward creators with tokens. Peer-to-peer (P2P) networks allow viewers to share video data directly with one another. Federated platforms, such as PeerTube, connect independently operated servers that share content through protocols like ActivityPub.

Decentralized video hosting works by storing videos across multiple servers or distributed networks. When users watch content, data can be delivered from different sources rather than one central provider.

However, not all decentralized YouTube alternatives are fully independent or censorship-resistant. Some still rely on centralized services for hosting, moderation, payments, or content discovery.

As the digital landscape evolves, several decentralized platforms are rising to the challenge, offering compelling alternatives to YouTube. Without further ado, here are our top seven decentralized YouTube alternatives.

DTube is a decentralized video-sharing platform that lets users upload, watch, and share videos without paying subscription fees. Unlike traditional video platforms, it uses peer-to-peer (P2P) technology to distribute video data between viewers, reducing reliance on centralized streaming infrastructure.

Its P2P model makes it an interesting alternative for users who value more distributed video delivery.

Odisee is an open-source video-sharing platform built on the LBRY protocol, a decentralized network for publishing and distributing digital content.

Unlike conventional video services, LBRY uses blockchain technology to register content claims and a peer-to-peer network to distribute files. Odysee provides a user-friendly interface for accessing videos and other digital media across this ecosystem.

Creators can publish videos, set prices for paid content, and receive tips or eligible viewing rewards in LBRY Credits (LBC). Publishing requires Credits, while watching content is generally free. Importantly, Odysee and LBRY are not interchangeable: Odysee is one application built on the broader LBRY protocol, and other compatible applications can access its content.

The platform offers users a range of options for creating, sharing, uploading, downloading, and purchasing content. It also rewards users with its own cryptocurrency, LBRY Credits (LBC), for various activities on the platform.

PeerTube is a free, open-source video-sharing platform that offers an alternative to centralized services like YouTube. Instead of relying on one company to host all content, it operates through independent servers called instances. These servers can connect and share videos through the ActivityPub protocol, allowing users to discover and interact with content across a federated network.

PeerTube supports video uploads, livestreaming, subscriptions, and community interaction. It also uses optional peer-to-peer streaming to reduce server bandwidth by allowing viewers to share video data.

The platform’s open-source architecture gives communities and organizations control over their own hosting environments, although content discovery and moderation can vary between instances.

3Speak is a decentralized YouTube alternative built on the Hive blockchain, designed to give creators greater ownership of their content.

Videos are published as posts under creators’ Hive accounts, while video files are stored on IPFS. This allows content to remain accessible through compatible Hive applications even if it is no longer displayed on the 3Speak website.

The platform offers long-form videos, Shorts, communities, and podcast RSS feeds. Creators can earn HIVE and HBD through community upvotes and receive a share of advertising revenue.

Viewers can also qualify for rewards by watching videos, podcasts or participating in content curation. These features make 3Speak particularly relevant to creators interested in blockchain-based publishing and alternative monetization models.

If you’ve ever used Netflix before (let’s be honest, everyone has) Flixxo’s UI will already be familiar to you.

Flixxo is a video platform focused on independent short films and microseries from emerging creators. Rather than competing directly with YouTube’s broad content library, it offers a curated entertainment experience featuring original stories and independent productions.

The platform focuses on helping creators distribute and monetize their work while giving viewers access to content from new voices. Its discovery model emphasizes editorial curation rather than relying entirely on recommendation algorithms.

Flixxo is therefore better suited to viewers interested in independent entertainment and short-form storytelling than those looking for a general-purpose video-sharing platform.

Traditional platforms like YouTube rely on centralized servers controlled by a single company. Decentralized video platforms distribute hosting and content delivery across independent servers, peer-to-peer (P2P) networks, or distributed storage systems.

Videos can be stored using technologies such as IPFS or hosted on independent servers. P2P networks allow viewers to share video data directly, reducing reliance on a single provider. Some platforms also use blockchain technology to record transactions, manage content ownership, and reward creators through cryptocurrency tokens.

Not every decentralized platform uses blockchain or crypto. Their technologies and monetization models vary depending on the underlying architecture.

Decentralized YouTube alternatives offer a new and innovative approach to video streaming, but like all technologies, they come with their own set of pros and cons.

With so many decentralized YouTube alternatives out there, it’s hard to know which are providing a quality service worth using. This guide aims to narrow your search and help you find a decentralized video platform that suits your needs.

Yes. Platforms like PeerTube, Odysee, and DTube offer alternatives to YouTube using decentralized technologies such as peer-to-peer networks, blockchain, or federated servers. They give users more control over content distribution and hosting.

It depends on your needs. PeerTube is a strong choice for open-source, federated video hosting, Odysee suits independent content publishing, while 3Speak focuses on blockchain-based creator rewards.

On decentralized YouTube alternatives, the platform is owned and governed by its users. These platforms are often backed by crypto economies that reward user engagement and interaction.

Yes. Some platforms support cryptocurrency rewards, tips, paid content, or advertising revenue sharing. For example, Odysee supports LBRY Credits (LBC), while 3Speak offers Hive-based rewards. Monetization options vary by platform.

They can be more resistant to centralized censorship because content is distributed across independent servers or networks. However, individual platform operators and server administrators can still moderate or remove content.

Some do, but not all. Odysee uses the LBRY blockchain ecosystem, while 3Speak integrates with Hive. PeerTube primarily relies on federation and peer-to-peer technology rather than blockchain.

Nevertheless, it is possible that the transfers do not actually indicate that MARA has sold BTC.

Meanwhile, MARA has also made it clear that it is no longer interested in holding all the Bitcoin it mines.

#5140. XRP Ledger closes loophole that could let token burners mint new supply

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The XRP Ledger has activated the fixCleanup3_4_0 amendment to close a security loophole in its permission delegation system.

Previously, an account given permission to burn tokens could also create new tokens under certain conditions. The fix blocks this unless the account also has permission to mint tokens.

The amendment also introduced fixes across several network features. These included Single Asset Vaults, lending, automated market makers (AMMs), the permissioned DEX, trust lines, NFTs, Multi-Purpose Tokens (MPTs), NFTs and escrow. Other fixes covered sponsorship, transaction signing, and permission delegation.

These fixes are mainly designed to address technical issues involving asset calculations, token transfers, liquidity pool operations, account permissions, and reserve requirements. For example, the AMM fix would now prevent liquidity providers from losing their pool tokens due to rounding errors during certain clawback operations.

Similarly, improvements to lending and vault operations would now result in accurate accounting and asset management. The update also boosts MPT transaction validation and resolves issues that could cause NFT offers to fail. All in all, the amendment aims to improve the reliability, security, and functionality of the XRP Ledger’s existing financial infrastructure.

That said, its activation followed two weeks of uninterrupted validator support, as XRPL amendments required more than 80% approval from trusted validators.

This comes as the XRP Ledger ranks ninth among networks in the RWA League Table, with $644.6 million in distributed tokenized assets, representing a 29.20% increase over the past month.

This followed a recent report from AMBCrypto, which indicated that XRPL ranked 10th in tokenized assets, with $642.5 million just a week ago.

Meanwhile, Santiment’s data for XRP Ledger suggests that its Total Open Interest fell from approximately $1.3 billion to below $1 billion towards the end of October. However, it subsequently witnessed a sharp recovery, reaching approximately $1.15 billion at press time.

At the same time, the Stablecoin Supply Ratio stood at 31.31 at the time of writing, marking a decline from around 34.5 to below 30 before recovering.

#5139. Treasury’s cash plan points to a possible December liquidity window for Bitcoin

Treasury’s November 4 plan frames a possible December respite, while tax receipts and a projected January cash peak complicate the reserve case.

Treasury’s new financing calendar gives Bitcoin’s liquidity hopes a possible December window, with projected cash peaks on either side. Its Nov. 4 refunding statement anticipates cash peaks near $1,025 billion in late November and late January, with a lower balance assumed at December’s close.

That sequence sharpens the timing of potential reserve relief. If Treasury spends down its account at the Federal Reserve, it can return reserves to banks, all else equal. But the planned December reduction in bill auction sizes partly reflects expected tax receipts, which move cash the other way. That makes December’s net cash movement the first test of the funding case.

In its refunding statement, Treasury estimates that the Treasury General Account, its operating account at the Fed, could peak at $1,025 billion, plus or minus $50 billion, in both late November 2026 and late January 2027. It assumes an $850 billion balance at the end of December.

The Nov. 2 borrowing estimates also assume a $1 trillion cash balance at the end of March 2027.

The bill schedule adds a seasonal mechanism. Treasury expects to maintain current benchmark bill auction sizes into late November and may issue a short-dated cash management bill around month-end. It then expects to reduce shorter-dated bill auction sizes during December, citing projected corporate and non-withheld tax receipts around the middle of the month.

January reverses that issuance direction: Treasury anticipates increasing auction sizes across the bill curve as seasonal fiscal outflows rise. It retains the ability to adjust those plans as borrowing needs change.

The paired cash peaks and bill sequence add timing within the refunding quarter to the quarter-end framework covered in CryptoSlate’s earlier Treasury analysis. A December respite could therefore sit inside a broader replenishment cycle rather than mark sustained easing.

Treasury projects late-November and late-January peaks around a lower assumed December close. Tax receipts complicate the bill-supply signal.

Payments from Treasury's Fed account support bank reserves, all else equal.

Tax receipts help explain December's expected bill reductions, while cash entering Treasury's account drains reserves, all else equal.

A cash drawdown can support reserves, all else equal. Lower bill auction sizes alone do not establish Bitcoin buying.

All future balances are projections or assumptions, not observations. Dates are not equally spaced in time. Tax receipts and other Fed balance-sheet changes can offset reserve support. No Bitcoin buying or price effect is established.

USD billions. Isolated checkpoints use a cash axis from $800 billion to $1,100 billion; peak whiskers show Treasury's stated plus or minus $50 billion range, not probabilities. No daily path is interpolated. Native selectable diagrams show all-else-equal accounting direction, not measured flows.

All future balances are projections or assumptions, not observations. Dates are not equally spaced in time. Tax receipts and other Fed balance-sheet changes can offset reserve support. No Bitcoin buying or price effect is established.

The Fed’s accounting explanation describes the reserve channel. Treasury payments transfer balances from its account into banks’ accounts at the central bank, increasing reserves, all else equal. Tax payments flowing into Treasury’s account drain those balances.

That distinction matters for December. Expected tax receipts help explain why Treasury can reduce bill auctions, but those receipts can also absorb reserves. Smaller auctions alone cannot establish that the combined cash flows will ease funding.

The realized net movement in Treasury’s account already incorporates financing, receipts and spending. Subtracting issuance again from that net cash change would count the financing effect twice. Fed securities purchases, lending and movements in currency, reverse repos or other deposits can also alter the final reserve total.

Nor should the refunding’s gross size be read as wholly new cash demand. Treasury plans to sell $125 billion against approximately $97 billion of privately held notes and bonds maturing Nov. 15, raising approximately $28 billion in new private cash. The three-year, ten-year and thirty-year auctions are scheduled for Nov. 9, 10 and 12, respectively. All settle Nov. 16, the relevant date for the cash exchange.

Treasury’s latest reported closing cash balance was $938 billion on Nov. 3, according to its operating-cash data available early Nov. 5. The Secured Overnight Financing Rate, or SOFR, was 3.97% on Nov. 3, the latest date available in that release. It measures overnight borrowing against Treasury collateral.

The Fed’s Oct. 29 balance-sheet release reports bank reserves of about $2,895 billion on Wednesday, Oct. 28. The cash, reserve and rate figures provide dated baselines rather than a synchronized signal that funding has eased.

For Bitcoin, the useful test begins with actual Treasury cash movements, followed by the reserve and funding outcome after other Fed balance-sheet changes. Whether easier funding translates into Bitcoin buying remains a separate, unproven step.

A realized December cash decline alongside steadier reserves and easier funding would strengthen the temporary-relief case. A cash drawdown offset elsewhere on the Fed’s balance sheet would weaken it. Any December improvement could face another test if Treasury rebuilds cash toward its projected late-January peak while increasing bill auction sizes.

#5138. Crédit Mutuel Arkéa Confirms Cyberattack After ZachXBT Ties It to $133M Crypto Theft

Crédit Mutuel Arkéa Confirms Cyberattack After ZachXBT Ties It to $133M Crypto Theft

French banking group Crédit Mutuel Arkéa has confirmed that it was hit by a cyberattack that allowed fraudulent operations, a day after onchain investigator ZachXBT named it as the alleged victim of a crypto theft of more than $133 million.

In a statement shared with Decrypt on Wednesday, the bank said the incident was "closed and now under control," and that operations were resuming gradually after it put stronger security measures in place. It said customers' assets were not affected, and that the attack had only delayed the execution of some transfers.

The bank did not mention crypto or say how much was taken. The amounts affected are still being assessed, but are "in any event not of a nature to call into question the solidity and financial health of the group," it said, according to Decrypt's translation.

Crédit Mutuel Arkéa said it informed the relevant supervisory authorities as soon as it detected the attack, and temporarily suspended some transactions as a precaution to prevent further fraudulent movements. It also warned customers to watch out for phishing attempts following the attack. Decrypt has reached out to Crédit Mutuel Arkéa for further comment, including on whether the losses involved crypto.

"Disclosing the EU financial institution exploited for $133M+ is allegedly Credit Mutuel Arkea, a French bank with $235B in assets," ZachXBT wrote on his Telegram channel on Tuesday. "The attack on the bank occurred for multiple days. I suspect the amount stolen is larger when accounting for OTC activity."

In an update on Tuesday, he said he had identified a further $27 million in exchange withdrawals by the attacker, bringing the total to more than $133 million, and that some of those withdrawals took place after his initial disclosure. He also published four Ethereum addresses he said were linked to the theft.

"My working theory is a large financial institution in Western Europe was exploited and it's currently being covered up," ZachXBT wrote at the time, accusing it of not having conducted "proper incident disclosure" and thereby preventing law enforcement from freezing some $30 million in stolen funds.

He did not say how he had identified the bank, or how the attacker gained access to its exchange accounts.

The mutual banking group has been preparing to move into crypto. In May, French financial newspaper L'Agefi reported that it had applied for a license under MiCA, the EU's crypto rulebook, to offer crypto services to its retail customers and to provide them as a white-label service through its Arkéa Banking Services division.

The allegation comes days after the $6.3 billion hack of Binance, the largest crypto theft on record, which the FBI has attributed to North Korean hackers. ZachXBT said the attacker in the suspected EU theft remains unidentified, but that he could say "with a high degree of confidence it is NOT Lazarus Group," the North Korean hacking collective, "based on multiple laundering indicators."

This story has been updated to add comment from Crédit Mutuel Arkéa.

#5137. Binance Revokes API Keys, Security Issue Found at SignalPlus

Binance has revoked all API keys associated with SignalPlus after the digital asset trading infrastructure provider identified a potential security vulnerability, prompting the world's largest cryptocurrency exchange to take measures to protect users, according to the latest report on the Binance's official Square feed.

The exchange confirmed Thursday that the decision was intended to prevent possible unauthorized access through connections established between Binance accounts and SignalPlus. Binance emphasized that its internal systems had not been compromised and that customer funds remained safe.

For now, the reason or the nature of the incident remains unclear. Both companies have yet to disclose how serious the potential vulnerability is or how many users may be affected. It is still unclear whether the attackers gained access to customer accounts or if the incident caused any financial losses.

The development underscores the security challenges associated with third-party infrastructure in cryptocurrency markets, where trading platforms increasingly depend on external applications for execution, analytics and automation.

Binance's security documentation advises users to revoke potentially compromised credentials immediately and restrict API permissions to necessary functions.

For affected SignalPlus users, restoring automated trading functionality may require generating new credentials once the integration is deemed secure.

SignalPlus provides trading infrastructure and analytical tools for cryptocurrency derivatives markets, allowing traders to manage positions and execute strategies across exchanges through API integrations.

These connections enable third-party applications to interact with exchange accounts without requiring users to transfer their assets off-platform. However, depending on the permissions granted, compromised API credentials can potentially expose accounts to unauthorized trading activity.

Binance has effectively severed existing integrations between its platform and SignalPlus. The measure could potentially disrupt automated trading strategies that rely on those connections.

The OG altcoin just formed an Inverse Head-and-Shoulders as big-time investors cash out from exchanges.

XRP is drawing attention after a sharp rise in exchange outflows coincided with whale accumulation and a developing inverse head-and-shoulders pattern. The setup points to tighter supply, but a breakout still needs confirmation on the chart.

Around 01:00 UTC, roughly 13.24 million XRP left exchanges. That kind of withdrawal reduces the amount of tokens sitting ready to sell. It does not guarantee higher prices on its own, but it shrinks the immediate supply that can hit the market if buyers appear.

2/5 Today, XRP exchange outflows spiked. Around 01:00 UTC, roughly 13.24 million $XRP were withdrawn from exchanges, potentially reducing the supply immediately available to sell. pic.twitter.com/PG3Y33f1Yx

Price has been consolidating near $1.30 while the outflow hit.

The largest wallets increased their holdings by about 60 million XRP over the past week. It is unclear whether those tokens came directly from the exchange withdrawals, but both metrics move in the same direction.

Large holders adding size while tokens leave exchanges is the classic combination that often precedes tighter supply.

On the daily chart, XRP price appears to be forming the right shoulder of a potential inverse head-and-shoulders bottom.

4/5 Now look at the daily chart: $XRP appears to be forming the right shoulder of a potential inverse head-and-shoulders bottom.I’m watching for a move toward the neckline at $1.60. A break above that resistance would confirm the pattern and put $2.10 in focus. pic.twitter.com/9bcXItpUlj

The neckline sits near $1.60. A clean break above it would confirm the pattern and put a measured target near $2.10 in focus.

Price is currently trading around $1.30 to $1.32, still well below that level, so the pattern remains a developing setup rather than a completed breakout.

Exchange outflows and whale buying strengthen the case by reducing available supply and showing demand from large holders. Neither data point forces a breakout.

The chart still needs to clear $1.60 before the inverse head-and-shoulders is confirmed and the $2.10 target comes into play.

Until then, the flows stay in a supportive background while price decides whether the pattern completes.

ApeChain is replacing LayerZero with Chainlink CCIP. This move comes as the layer 3 blockchain aims to strengthen its infrastructure and improve connectivity across the Yuga Labs ecosystem.

The migration makes CCIP the official cross-chain standard for ApeChain, Bored Ape Yacht Club applications, and APE.

Ultimately, Rising transfers, repeat activity, deeper liquidity, and competitive fees will show whether adoption delivers lasting growth.

Additionally, integrations with Aave [AAVE], Lombard, and Venus demonstrate CCIP’s continued involvement in the development of DeFi. While CCIP continues to add to its involvement in DeFi infrastructure, commercial returns for the project remain limited.

ApeChain has adopted Chainlink CCIP as its cross-chain standard for APE and other ecosystem assets.

Circle reported 17% revenue growth and an earnings beat, but stripping out ARC presales reveals weaker underlying performance despite soaring USDC adoption.

Circle's $182 million ARC token presale drove its third-quarter earnings surprise even as its core USDC reserve income declined.

Its traditional reserve business remained under pressure despite growing stablecoin adoption. Reserve income fell 8% to $658 million as the average yield on USDC reserves declined by 61 basis points to 3.5%, offsetting an 8% increase in average circulation.

#5133. Wall Street’s Plumbing Goes Onchain as Morgan Stanley Tokenizes Securities

Wall Street’s Plumbing Goes Onchain as Morgan Stanley Tokenizes Securities

Morgan Stanley has started moving U.S. stocks and Treasuries onto blockchain rails, using Wall Street’s own plumbing to do it.

The bank said Tuesday that it’s among the first to tokenize real-world assets through the DTCC Tokenization Service, converting U.S. equities and Treasuries into tokenized form.

The service, which went live Oct. 28, is run by the Depository Trust Co., a subsidiary of the Depository Trust & Clearing Corp. The DTC is the post-trade backbone of U.S. markets, with more than $114 trillion in assets under custody.

“The hands-on experience of integrating tokenized securities with established market infrastructure will inform the continued development of the firm’s digital asset capabilities as the market evolves,” said Amy Oldenburg, Morgan Stanley’s head of digital assets strategy.

The service lets eligible firms convert securities held at DTC into tokenized “digital twins” and back again, delivering them to digital wallets of their choice. The tokens keep the same ownership rights, investor protections and economic entitlements as the originals, according to DTCC, and are designed to let firms move collateral and tap new pools of liquidity within minutes. The service currently runs on the Canton Network and DTCC’s private Besu network, with more blockchains planned for 2027.

Morgan Stanley is one of 20 DTC participants that have signed up so far, alongside Bank of America, Citi, J.P. Morgan, Fidelity Investments, Citadel Securities, and UBS, among others. DTCC said it developed the service with input from more than 150 firms.

The launch caps a regulatory path that began in December 2025, when the SEC gave DTC a no-action letter allowing it to tokenize Russell 1000 stocks, major index ETFs and U.S. Treasuries. DTCC has also worked with Chainlink on tokenized collateral management and laid out its plans for the service earlier this year as major Wall Street firms signed on.

The bank-led approach contrasts with crypto-native efforts to tokenize stocks. Fifty-six companies, including Tesla, SpaceX, Amazon, and Nvidia, have objected to having their shares traded on OKXICE TSV, a planned tokenized stock venue from OKX and NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange. Under the SEC’s innovation exemption, those objections bar the venue from listing third-party tokenized versions of their stock.

Demand is building, but the market is still young. The International Monetary Fund found last month that tokenized stocks trade mostly outside U.S. market hours, though they remain more volatile and less liquid than their traditional counterparts.

#5131. Autonomous AI Hacking? Ripple CTO Emeritus Says He Picked 'Right Time to Retire'

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

According to one of the architects of the XRP Ledger, now Ripple CTO Emeritus David Schwartz, his retirement from active development almost a year ago came at the right time. "I picked a great time to retire!", said Schwartz, reacting to the latest report from OpenAI researchers, which touches on testing a new cyber model. Just one autonomous run and the AI completely changed the familiar standards of network security, OpenAI researcher Eric Wallace confirms.

The report shows how OpenAI's pipeline works. First thing to understand, it's a fully complete cycle: the system independently finds a potential bug, verifies it, writes a working exploit to confirm it and automatically releases a patch. This was tested on the Linux kernel, the core software that runs most of the planet's servers (including OpenAI's own compute clusters).

The Web3 sphere is one where open-source code is the foundation of blockchain security, so this development is a challenge to say the least. If AI finds zero-day vulnerabilities in a matter of hours, manual code auditing by humans becomes useless.

Yes, Schwartz stepped down from the post of Ripple CTO into honorary retirement last November, but he hasn't abandoned his terminal. Just recently he showed monitoring charts of his personal server hub on the XRP Ledger network. There, a node is running under colossal load: it maintains more than 550 active connections (peers), shows latency of around 200ms and processes about 110,000 incoming messages per second.

Besides, Schwartz continues personal work on the network's internal architectural problems. In a discussion with active validators, Schwartz detailed a transaction queue bug that caused the local load metric on his server to jump to 540, triggering fee spikes within the ledger.

Schwartz picked the "right time" to step back from Ripple, but he's clearly not out of the game.

#5130. India warns AI-assisted hackers are stealing crypto wallet keys

The CRAFTY FALCON campaign targets financial firms’ own infrastructure, while crypto service providers face stolen wallet keys and siphoned funds.

India’s Computer Emergency Response Team, CERT-In, has issued a critical warning about escalating AI-assisted attacks on banking, financial services and insurance entities, including repeat targeting after incomplete remediation. Its Nov. 4 CRAFTY FALCON advisory describes an ongoing campaign that abuses financial institutions’ own cloud services and payment interfaces to move funds.

Cryptocurrency exchanges and virtual digital asset service providers are among the affected categories. CERT-In reports stolen wallet keys and funds siphoned from cryptocurrency wallets, placing crypto custody within a broader warning that also covers non-bank financial companies, digital lending platforms, fintech firms and payment providers. The public advisory does not name an affected exchange or quantify the losses.

The notice updates CERT-In’s Oct. 9 advisory on unauthorized fund transfers through payment and disbursement APIs, the interfaces applications use to request those transactions. @IndianCERT announced the new advisory on Nov. 4. The agency says its finance response team, CSIRT-Fin, has designated the actor behind the campaign CRAFTY FALCON.

CERT-In says it continues to receive reports of successful attacks involving NBFCs and fintech entities that had received earlier restricted warnings and undertaken remediation. It specifically identifies re-targeting after incomplete remediation, with attacks timed for holidays, weekends and other periods of reduced monitoring.

CERT-In describes several routes through institutions’ infrastructure. Internet-facing testing and staging environments have provided entry points, followed by access to production systems through shared credentials, tokens or network connections. Modified application packages have also been used to initiate unauthorized API calls, defeating controls that assumed requests would come only from the legitimate application.

Cloud management channels are another reported avenue. The advisory says services including AWS Systems Manager and Lambda were abused to execute commands and deploy malicious files, allowing hostile activity to blend into legitimate administration.

Even restricting access to approved IP addresses can leave an opening. CERT-In says attackers originated traffic from shared third-party fetcher pools that institutions had allow-listed. It recommends replacing that reliance with controls that authenticate the requesting party, such as mutual TLS, cryptographically signed requests or scoped API keys on dedicated vendor endpoints.

The agency also describes micro-value test transactions, such as one rupee, used to check unauthorized fund flows before larger transfers. Merchant settlement APIs used by payment aggregators were exploited or abused as well.

CERT-In attributes the campaign’s AI assistance to its incident analysis. It identifies publicly available offensive-security tools including ARTEX, PentAGI, Cairn and CyberStrikeAI, alongside rapid production of tailored scripts and payloads that adapt to target responses. Those descriptions are the agency’s assessment of the activity.

The public advisory names no affected exchange and quantifies no losses. The wallet-key route is shown separately; no common intrusion path, exhaustive attack sequence or protection guarantee is inferred.

CERT-In calls for heightened round-the-clock monitoring, with more coverage during weekends, holidays and other periods when an attack could go unnoticed longer.

For institutions recovering from an incident, the advisory recommends forensic verification that the intrusion has been eradicated, followed by continued threat hunting.

The technical controls include separating testing environments from production, restricting cloud privileges, rapidly patching exposed applications and enforcing transaction rules on the server. Signed API requests add another check on whether a payment instruction should be accepted.

Transaction monitoring should also connect small test payments with subsequent high-value transfers.

For crypto service providers, CERT-In recommends storing wallet keys in hardware security modules or cold custody, requiring multi-signature approvals for withdrawals and monitoring withdrawal patterns for anomalies. Keys should be rotated immediately when compromise is suspected.

CERT-In says it has shared the full indicators of compromise, infrastructure fingerprints and detection rules with BFSI entities through restricted advisories. Its public warning cautions against limiting the response to blocking indicators, alongside its recommendations for continued threat hunting and verified eradication.

XRP just formed a new short-term golden cross versus Bitcoin, and the market is weighing whether the technical signal can underpin more upside.

A golden cross is when a shorter-term moving average crosses above a longer-term moving average. In this case, the 50 MA crossing above the 200 MA.

This signal appearing on the XRP/BTC 3-hour chart might suggest improving momentum and a possible short-term trend change.

The development is especially important for XRP, since it could propel renewed strength against Bitcoin. On a 24-hour basis, XRP is outperforming Bitcoin in percentage gains, up 2.19% while Bitcoin was up 1.74% in the same timeframe. However, a golden cross is not a guarantee of sustained upside, and traders will be watching whether price action confirms the signal.

In a recent X post, Ali, a crypto analyst, pointed to a spike in XRP exchange outflows. He said that around 01:00 UTC today, about 13.24 million XRP were withdrawn from exchanges, potentially reducing the immediate supply for sale.

At the same time, some of the biggest whales have been buying, increasing their holdings by nearly 60 million XRP in the last week.

Likewise, the XRP Binance Scarcity Index has achieved its highest level since 2022. This signals growing scarcity of XRP on Binance amid a decline in the amount of supply available for trading compared with previous periods.

Ali stated that he is watching for a move toward $1.60, as a break above this resistance would put $2.10 in focus. Exchange outflows and whale accumulation are building the bullish case for XRP, but the charts will decide whether it becomes a breakout.

In separate news, t54 Labs has announced a partnership with Altair to bring AI model-token bill settlement to the XRP Ledger. Altair is now an x402 merchant on XRPL AI Hub, with a daily-invoice resource that accepts settlement in RLUSD.

#5127. Bitcoin Rebounds After Democratic Midterm Sweep, But Crypto Faces Tougher Fight in Washington

Bitcoin Rebounds After Democratic Midterm Sweep, But Crypto Faces Tougher Fight in Washington

Bitcoin and Ethereum recovered Wednesday after Democrats won control of both houses of Congress in the U.S. midterm elections, despite the results likely making the crypto industry's push for friendlier federal rules more difficult.

The crypto market recovery, in technical terms, appears to be more a natural bounce, and in line with expectations, instead of a trend reversal or a FOMO-driven spike. The overnight selloff also reversed as U.S. stock futures bounced back, making it hard to pin the recovery on politics alone.

Bitcoin briefly hit a six-week low before a bounce that took it back to just above $80,000 once again, above its average price of the last 50 days, which is a solid bullish sign after weeks of bearish correction.

Ethereum, meanwhile, spiked 7% to $2,434, while U.S. stocks on the whole jumped around 1.6%, with the Dow Jones Industrial Average leading the way with a 2.5% rally.

Analysts who spoke to Decrypt said the Democratic sweep had largely been priced into crypto markets before election night. A rebound in Bitcoin therefore says less about investors welcoming the election result than about how much bad news they were already expecting.

The industry has spent more than $300 million supporting crypto-friendly candidates from both parties across the 2024 and 2026 election cycles. About $176 million went toward more than 100 races this year alone.

That spending was aimed partly at getting legislation through Congress that would give digital asset businesses clearer rules. The Clarity Act, a major piece of that effort, would define how federal regulators oversee different types of crypto assets and trading activity.

The bill was in trouble before Tuesday's election. Senators voted 49-50 against advancing it in September, falling short of the 60 votes needed to move past a procedural hurdle. That was not a final vote on the legislation, but it stopped the measure from reaching a full debate.

Negotiations had already run into disputes over restrictions on government officials' crypto business interests and the treatment of rewards paid on stablecoins, tokens designed to hold a fixed value. President Donald Trump's personal and family crypto ventures became a particular point of conflict for Democratic lawmakers.

Democratic control of Congress makes a quick return to the same legislative deal less likely, but it does not automatically undo what the industry secured under Trump. His administration has eased enforcement, established a strategic Bitcoin reserve and overseen the passage of a federal stablecoin law.

The distinction matters more for exchanges, token issuers, and other firms seeking clear legal permissions than for Bitcoin's day-to-day price. Analysts told Decrypt that liquidity, interest rates and investment flows still exert a stronger influence on the cryptocurrency itself.

The broader crypto market has shed roughly $300 billion over the past month, bringing its total value to about $2.67 trillion. Rising borrowing costs and worries about returns on artificial intelligence investments have pushed traders toward less risky assets like bonds or gold.

Major Bitcoin exchange-traded funds, which offer exposure to the asset through traditional brokerage accounts, recorded $215 million in net withdrawals in October. That brought their year-to-date net outflows to $1.84 billion.

The new Congress takes office in January 2027. Unless lawmakers revive the Clarity Act before the current session ends, the industry will need to start a new legislative effort with Democrats controlling both chambers.

#5126. Zcash (ZEC) Co-Founder Issues Warning on AI Security Threat

Zcash co-founder and computer scientist Eli Ben-Sasson has issued a crucial warning about crypto security amid recent hacker attacks on crypto wallets.

In his latest post on X, Ben-Sasson wrote that the crypto industry faces a key threat besides quantum computers. In particular, he pointed to the growing capabilities of AI as a tool for cybersecurity attacks.

The reason is that today, more than ever before, we rely on cryptography.

"Your crypto keys, your online life, any app that holds something important to you — it's all secured by cryptography," he wrote.

According to Ben-Sasson, the growing power of AI tools puts more pressure on wallet cryptography. This threat is more likely to become an issue before quantum computers pose a significant threat.

"This quick rise in cybersecurity attacks is not going to slow down, not with AI becoming so powerful," Ben-Sasson wrote.

The Zcash co-founder also pointed out the need for stronger cryptographic standards. He specifically highlighted hash-based methods such as ZK-STARKs.

The discussion comes weeks after the infamous Binance hack. The incident resulted in around $6.3 billion in losses from the exchange's hot wallets.

Binance temporarily suspended withdrawals while investigating the breach. The exchange has since reported recovering around 95% of the affected assets.

AI hacker attacks have been on the rise in 2026. According to Google Threat Intelligence statistics, the monthly number of disclosed software vulnerabilities has nearly doubled over the past year.

This means that AI has already become a powerful scanner for potential system weaknesses.

For instance, Casa CSO Jameson Lopp pointed out that attackers can already use AI to automate the search for conventional software bugs and weaknesses in wallet firmware.

In other words, the discussion should be focused on more specific security vulnerabilities in existing software rather than a hypothetical future AI threat.

#5124. Strategy’s $100 STRC comeback could turn a cash drain into fresh Bitcoin buying power

After about $2 billion in buybacks, STRC reached the price at which Strategy could sell new shares to help finance Bitcoin purchases.

Strategy's STRC preferred stock briefly returned to $100 on Nov. 4, potentially restoring a funding channel for Bitcoin purchases after months of cash-consuming share buybacks.

The company announced on Nov. 4 that its STRC security had reached $100, prompting executive chairman Michael Saylor to declare:

The recovery followed about $2 billion in share repurchases since July, as Strategy sought to support the preferred stock after its prolonged decline below its $100 stated value.

Independent tracker STRC.live reported that the security touched $100 before retreating to close at $99.97. It also estimated that renewed at-the-market (ATM) activity may have financed the purchase of approximately 191 BTC on Wednesday.

Strategy has not confirmed those purchases.

Strategy's Bitcoin treasury stood at 849,327 BTC as of Nov. 1.

The $100 threshold matters because Strategy’s current policy is not to issue STRC below that level. Its Nov. 2 disclosure listed about $18 billion of STRC available for issuance and sale as of Nov. 1. New sales could help finance Bitcoin purchases without issuing additional common stock.

However, months of below-par trading constrained that approach, forcing Strategy to shift from raising capital through STRC to repurchasing the security at a discount.

STRC's rebound followed a significant change to its dividend structure, with Strategy introducing daily distributions from Nov. 1 to improve the security's price stability and appeal to income-seeking investors.
